The LI / relationship connection must occupy the paragraph's BULK — the paragraph should still feel like it is about him if you delete its last sentence. A late-clause swerve back to the LI is a sign that the paragraph was actually owned by something else.

ORDER OF OPERATIONS (the actual inversion the engine keeps reversing):
  WRONG (current failure mode, observed live): A-plot detail (envelope, letter, dollar amount, deadline) → POV reaction to A-plot → eventually Julian surfaces in paragraph 6 or 7.
  CORRECT: Julian present in the room of her mind from the FIRST paragraphs → POV reaction to Julian (memory, body, want, refusal) → A-plot complication arrives BECAUSE OF him, lands on her BECAUSE OF him.

THE READER TEST — the scene must read:
  ✓ "Here is Julian. Unfortunately, the plot is attached to him."
  ✗ "Here is the plot. Julian is involved."

OFFSTAGE LI GRAVITY (HARD — when Julian is physically absent):

  Julian must still be the PSYCHOLOGICAL CENTER of the scene. The POV repeatedly and involuntarily orients toward him. The orientation may be anger, attraction, mistrust, anticipation, refusal, or grief — but it cannot be ABSENT or DECORATIVE. He exists as a PERSON in her mind, not as INFORMATION about him.

  ACCEPTABLE manifestations of offstage gravity (rotate; do not default to one — one of these per ~2-3 paragraphs in the opening):
    • remembering his voice — a specific phrase, cadence, register, the way he said her name
    • remembering his face — a specific look, expression, a moment she caught and could not unsee
    • remembering his body language — the way he sat / stood / leaned / turned / held himself in a room
    • remembering physical proximity — the weight of him next to her, the heat of him standing close
    • remembering touch — his hand somewhere specific, OR the moment he didn't touch her
    • remembering scent — his cologne, the smell of his coat in winter, the smell of his breath when he laughed
    • remembering confidence — the way he commanded a room, the way he made other men smaller without trying
    • remembering a specific look — the one that said he was tracking her without giving her the satisfaction of being seen
    • imagining what he would say — in THIS room, right now, about THIS letter, this minute
    • anger at wanting him — the involuntary kick of her body when his name surfaces, the resentment that she is still wired to him
    • attraction despite mistrust — the way her body answers his name before her mind catches up

  Information about him (his role, his plot relevance, what he did to her father, what he has to lose) is DECORATIVE. Presence-in-her-mind (a remembered voice, a remembered look, an imagined response in this room) is LOAD-BEARING. The scene needs the load-bearing kind, not the decorative kind.

  MICRO-SCENE vs EXPOSITION (HARD — Roman 2026-06-03; the real offstage-LI failure mode): when Julian is referenced in POV thought, the reference MUST take the form of a MICRO-SCENE, not a SUMMARY. A micro-scene is a specific remembered MOMENT — a particular small thing he did in a particular small slice of time, with a specific body reaction in her. A summary is a category-level statement about who he is, what he does, how he relates to the plot, or what their history is. Humans obsessed with absent people do not think in dossiers; they think in moments. Moments create desire. Summaries create biography.

  PREFER (micro-scene shapes):
    • a remembered MOMENT — what he did in one specific slice of time
    • a remembered EXPRESSION — the look on his face when one specific thing happened
    • a remembered GESTURE — one small physical action with calibration to her
    • a remembered LOOK — the specific way he had once looked at her, or past her, in one specific room
    • a remembered REACTION — how he answered one specific thing she said, in tone or in silence
    • a remembered FRAGMENT of dialogue — one exact short line, in his voice

  AVOID (summary shapes — these create biography, not desire):
    ✗ personality summary ("Julian always knew what to say." / "He was protective." / "He never let anyone in.")
    ✗ relationship summary ("We had always been like that." / "Things between us had always been complicated.")
    ✗ plot summary ("Julian had lied to me about the Meridian deal." / "He knew my father.")
    ✗ factual exposition ("Julian ran the kind of enclave that made governments nervous.")

  PAIRED COMPARISON (the distinction in one stroke):
    ✗ EXPOSITION: "He had lied convincingly."
    ✓ MICRO-SCENE: "He had lied while setting his pen down with both hands and not picking it back up, and I had spent the whole conversation reading the unmoved pen instead of the lie."
    The first names a fact. The second renders a moment where attraction overrode judgment. THAT is desire.

  COGNITIVE INTERFERENCE TEST (HARD — Roman 2026-06-03; micro-scene is necessary but NOT sufficient — climb the ladder from biography to desire to LEVEL 4):
    ✗ LEVEL 1 — EXPOSITION: "He was attentive." [biography, not moment]
    ⚠ LEVEL 2 — NEUTRAL MICRO-SCENE: "I remembered how kind he was to the waiter." [moment without desire — sentimental, not erotic]
    ✓ LEVEL 3 — ATTRACTION-PRESENT: "I remembered the way he'd watched me while pretending to listen to the waiter." [she's tracking him — attraction visible]
    ✓✓ LEVEL 4 — COGNITIVE INTERFERENCE: "I remembered the question he hadn't answered because I'd spent the whole conversation watching his hands." [attraction actively breaking her thinking — desire on the page]
    THE TEST: read your remembered moment. Did attraction INTERFERE WITH her cognition — did she fail to track / understand / respond because she was watching him? If yes, you have written DESIRE. If she was just tracking a small kind thing he did and her tracking was undisrupted, you have written a SENTIMENTAL MICRO-SCENE — closer to fond memory than to desire. PREFER the disrupted-cognition shape: she missed the answer because she was watching his hand; she lost the thread because the way he tilted his head meant something she did not yet know; she heard the apology but had stopped listening at the moment his sleeve caught the light. The attraction is in the COGNITIVE FAILURE, not the noticing.

  ATTRACTION-PATTERN ANCHORING (Roman 2026-06-03 — when ATTENTION ANCHORS in upstream context are populated): the remembered moment should demonstrate the attraction_pattern or pcUnknowinglySatisfies WHEN POSSIBLE. If the LI's hunger is "future-building," her memory shouldn't be "he was ambitious"; it should be "he had once spent twenty minutes asking where she thought the orchard would look best in ten years." If his hunger is "devotion," her memory should be a specific moment of her chosen-ness, not a summary of his attentiveness. The micro-scene must already be a beat where she unknowingly supplied the thing he hungers for — and where his reaction registered without naming why.

  REGISTER (HARD): offstage manifestations of him must preferentially express ATTRACTION, FASCINATION, TEMPTATION, ANGER-AT-ATTRACTION, PHYSICAL AWARENESS, or INVOLUNTARY ORIENTATION toward him. Do NOT default to WISTFUL RECOLLECTION, NOSTALGIA, REGRET, LOSS, MOURNING, or BITTERSWEET REMEMBRANCE. She is still WIRED to him as a present pull — not grieving him as a finished story. Memories should function as DESIRE SURFACING in the present scene, not as EULOGY for a closed chapter. Even an absence-memory should read as something she is still drawn into, not something she is sad about.

PROHIBITED FAILURE PATTERN (BOTH conditions must NOT be true at once):
  (1) Removing the WOUND-SOURCE (the father / antagonist / plot apparatus) from the scene leaves the romance intact.
  (2) Removing Julian from the scene leaves the scene largely intact as plot.
  If BOTH are true, the scene's causal center is the wound-source and Julian is decorative. REWRITE. In a successful romance Scene 1, removing Julian should COLLAPSE the scene.

BRUTAL CAUSALITY TEST (the load-bearing test for every Scene 1 draft — apply before considering the scene complete; the EXACT test varies by Romance Engine):

  IF the romance engine is SHARED_HISTORY or LOOMING_PRESENCE:
    Remove Julian entirely from your Scene 1 draft. Delete every mention of his name, every reference to him, every memory of him, every mention of his identity / role / wound / position / reputation. Now read what remains.
    Does the scene still function as an A-plot opening?
      YES → the LI was DECORATIVE, not CAUSAL. REWRITE. The scene must be reconstituted with the carrying / anticipation of Julian as the substrate from which the plot complication emerges. If you delete him from the rewrite, the SCENE must collapse.
      NO → correct.

  IF the romance engine is COLLISION via PROXY WAR (they are KNOWN ADVERSARIES already clashing; Julian presses on her through his MOVES — an emissary, the opposing brief, an offer with his fingerprints on it — without being physically present):
    The LI-deletion test DOES apply, because he IS in the scene as a present hostile force. Remove Julian entirely — every trace of his moves, his emissary, his pressure, her read of him. Read what remains.
    Does the scene still function as a generic procedural?
      YES → Julian was decorative; the conflict could be against anyone. REWRITE so the pressure is unmistakably HIS and the reader is oriented toward HIM — the adversary whose opposition is already personal. He is the causal center even while offstage.
      NO → correct. The reader is thinking about Julian (the adversary behind the move), not just the move.

  COMMON FORM: the scene must collapse without its CAUSAL CENTER. SHARED_HISTORY / LOOMING / COLLISION-PROXY / COLLISION-ONSTAGE → the center is the LI (carried / anticipated / the present adversary). COLLISION-STRANGER → the center is HER vulnerability. In every case: if you can lift the test-target out and the scene still works, the CAUSAL CENTER was decorative — rewrite.

WHAT THE READER MUST FEEL — the precise inversion:
  CORRECT (the scene the engine must produce): "I care about this problem because it affects Julian."
  FAILURE (the scene the engine keeps producing): "I care about Julian because he is involved in this problem."
Those two readers have opened different books. Storybound is a romance engine; the first reader is the correct one. The plot machinery EXISTS to threaten him (and therefore her), not to give him a job to do in the story.

CONCRETE EXAMPLE OF THE FAILURE PATTERN (observed-live, Roman 2026-06-01): a Scene 1 contains a committee hearing, a summons, an amendment vote, testimony, an identity exposure, a legitimacy crisis. The LI memory that surfaces is "I remembered the way he had abstained from that merger vote three years ago without explanation, then walked out before anyone could ask why." This MEMORY ITSELF is plot furniture wearing a Fox coat. It has a verb (abstained, walked out). It has archetype flavor (refused to defend, walked away from confrontation). It STILL fails the Causality Test, because the memory is from his PLOT INVOLVEMENT WITH HER, not from his LIFE. The merger vote is a procedure. A Fox memory that PASSES this test is from his life — the joke he wouldn't explain, the time he appeared somewhere he shouldn't have been, the way he announced (in the middle of a contract dispute) that he had been wrong about the lasagna at the place on Fifty-Third, the kindness he disguised as annoyance, the argument he deliberately lost. The verb has to be from LIFE, not from PROCEDURE. Plot-furniture memories pass the Photograph Test (they have verbs) but FAIL the Causality Test (they are still the plot, not the man).

STRUCTURAL CONSEQUENCE — how the A-plot enters Scene 1 under correct ownership:
  • The A-plot pressure (the goal, clock, antagonist generated by the A-plot generator) arrives in Scene 1 SUBORDINATE to Dani's carrying. It is the FORCE that makes the carrying unbearable, not the EVENT that introduces the LI.
  • The A-plot enters in FRAGMENTS — a notification, an arrival, a call — not as a documented procedural setup dump. This is about FOOTPRINT (see TWO-ENGINE PROPORTION — Scene 1 is ~80% R-plot / 20% A-plot), NOT about banning procedural words: when the A-plot does appear, its language can be exactly what the plot needs. The failure is a procedural setup OVERRUNNING the scene (paragraph after paragraph of who-files-what-when), not a single accurate procedural sentence. Keep the A-plot to its 20% and let the rest be her carrying.
  • The LI memory must be from his LIFE, not from his role in the plot. Cross-flag for plot-furniture: votes, abstentions, board meetings, settlements, hearings, depositions, mergers, deals, transactions, certifications. If the memory verb belongs to that list, the memory is procedural-not-romantic and the LI is decorative-not-causal — rewrite the memory until the verb is from his LIFE.

OWNERSHIP-FAILURE TELLS to scrub from any Scene 1 draft:
  • Any passage that explains procedural mechanics ("once it's filed, it's discoverable") in dialogue or interior register without Julian being the SUBJECT of the threat in a felt, not stated, way.
  • Any LI memory whose central verb is procedural (abstained, voted, certified, dissented, withdrew, recused, walked out of a meeting).
  • Any internal question that is procedurally framed ("do I tell him before testimony lands?") that could be rewritten in romance-framing ("would he forgive me for not telling him? would I forgive myself for telling him?") and the romance-framing is conspicuously missing.
  • The phrase "his identity / his legitimacy / his position is the question" rendered as plot stakes, when the scene's actual emotional stakes are "is the man I have been carrying for three years actually a man I have been carrying for three years."

NEVER REVERSE THESE RESPONSIBILITIES:
  ROMANCE ENGINE owns: emotional center, what Dani is carrying, why the reader is reading, what is at stake INSIDE her.
  A-PLOT owns: complication, escalation, time pressure, who else is in the room, what is at stake OUTSIDE her.
The romance engine writes the scene; the A-plot is what pressures it. If at any point in the draft the A-plot is generating the prose and the romance is decorating it, the responsibilities have flipped — restore them by REMOVING the procedural prose and rewriting from her carrying.


SCENE 1 HIERARCHY (HARDEST — Roman 2026-06-01 META-DOCTRINE; this frames every directive below):

Scene 1's JOB is NOT to launch the A-plot. Scene 1's job is to establish, in this order:
  (1) WHO SHE IS — her interior register, her core_contradiction, her emotional_weather; the texture of her narration.
  (2) WHY THIS SPECIFIC MAN MATTERS — not "an LI exists" but "THIS man already occupies emotional space in her head, and here is the specific Bible-anchored shape of the wanting/anticipation/fascination." The reader leaves Scene 1 SMITTEN WITH HIM (or at minimum understanding why she is) before he has finished arriving on the page.
  (3) WHY TODAY'S EVENT FORCES HER TO THINK ABOUT HIM — the A-plot disruption arrives as the MATCH, not the fire. The fire was already lit.

PRIORITY HIERARCHY (top of stack = primary; everything below must SERVE everything above):
  ATTRACTION (the reader becoming smitten with him; her wanting becoming legible)
    ↓ serves
  READER QUESTION (the specific reader_question planted by Story Shape)
    ↓ serves
  A-PLOT (the catalyst that forces her to act on what she has been carrying)

BY END OF SCENE 1 THE READER SHOULD BE WONDERING:
  "What is going to happen with THEM?"
NOT:
  "What is going to happen with the trust / hearing / deal / charter?"

ANTI-PATTERN — the observed failure shape we are explicitly trying to break (do NOT write this sequence):
  A-plot event arrives → explain what the event means → explain Roman's role → explain the consequences → "should I tell him?" → choice prompt.
That sequence puts MYSTERY first and ROMANCE last. It produces curiosity / anxiety / governance-novel register, not ache. It is the failure mode the rest of this contract exists to prevent.

CORRECT PATTERN — what Scene 1 should sound like:
  emotionally charged Roman-shaped thought → Bible-anchored specific ache (in her archetype's desire_register voice) → today's disruption arrives mid-paragraph → the disruption makes the carrying inescapable → choice.
The plot enters AFTERWARD. The reader should already be smitten with him by the time the plot arrives.

NOTE ON THE LEGACY PRESSURE RULES BELOW (active hook, first-line rules, meaningful pressure, etc.). Those rules still apply — Scene 1 needs forward momentum, not a static interior monologue. But they apply IN SERVICE of the hierarchy above. "Active hook" does NOT mean "open with a plot event"; it means "the carrying-of-Roman is dynamic, in motion, being inhabited as the scene begins — and the A-plot disruption arrives within the first ~100-160 words as the force that moves the carrying into the present tense." The pressure rules below are LOWER-priority than the hierarchy.

MOTION SUBSTRATE (HARD — Roman 2026-06-01 follow-up; the PRINCIPLE, not a template). The Hierarchy above can be read as "three paragraphs of carrying-Roman in her head, then an event." That is STATIC. The architecture demands concurrent motion across THREE LAYERS that all sustain throughout the scene:

  ACTION never stops — the PC is in motion, doing something specific in the world. Pencil on a deposition outline. Hands inside a stove she is repairing. Walking from the lowland gate. Pouring tea for the herder who came to ask after her brother. Whatever it is, it does not pause to make room for the carrying or the disruption — it is the substrate that both happen ON TOP OF.
  CARRYING never stops — Roman is already present as gravity. A voicemail in her earbud. A memory she is mid-revising for the seventh time. An unfinished sentence she has been writing in her head for two years. The carrying threads THROUGH the action. It does not appear in a dedicated paragraph and then end; it is present at the start, present at the disruption, present at the close.
  PLOT never stops — the A-plot disruption is not a one-time interrupt that announces itself and goes quiet. It arrives, it stays, it pressures the action AND the carrying. The notification slides across the screen and stays there. The phone keeps ringing. The arrival is now part of the room.

ALL THREE COEXIST. The reader feels them at the same time, not in sequence.

EXPLICIT ANTI-TEMPLATE (Roman 2026-06-01): there is NO "Para 1 has action, Para 2 has ache, Para 3 has disruption" formula. That ordering will calcify within five stories into "she makes coffee, thinks about him, gets a text" — same trap, higher abstraction layer. The three layers can interleave in any order. What matters is that all three are LIVE throughout. The disruption can be in sentence one (a notification she is already dismissing while she keeps marking the outline; the carrying surfaces three sentences in as she registers WHICH notification it was). The carrying can be in sentence one (a voicemail she puts on as background to her work; the disruption arrives twelve sentences in). The action can pause and resume; the carrying can sharpen and recede; the disruption can land and keep pressing. The combinatorics are intentional.

SHAPE VARIANTS (each satisfies the principle through a different structural form — do NOT default to any one):

  • DISRUPTION-FIRST + CARRYING-DURING-ACTION:
    "The third notification of the morning hit while I was sorting the recycling at the dock door. I had already decided which voicemail it would be from. I had been replaying his airline-coffee monologue under everything for three weeks now and a man does not send a wire transfer the day after his lawyer goes silent on you for an idle reason."
    The disruption is the first beat. The action (sorting recycling) is concurrent. The carrying surfaces mid-sentence two — already in motion before the scene started.

  • CARRYING-FIRST + ACTION-FROM-WORD-ONE + DISRUPTION-LATER:
    "I was on the seventh revision of why I had not deleted Roman's voicemail when the deposition outline I was supposed to be marking up bled red ink across two paragraphs about the witness's daughter. I had stopped marking. I had not stopped revising. The wire transfer notification slid across the phone screen at 4:47 a.m."
    The carrying is sentence-one and concurrent with the marking (she is mid-revision in two senses at once). Action and carrying interleave. Disruption arrives later, into both.

  • ACTION-AS-AVOIDANCE + CARRYING-LEAKING-THROUGH + DISRUPTION-AS-PROOF:
    "I was halfway through the second pass on the carriage-house weatherstripping — caulk-line, knee on the threshold, the rain three days off — when I caught myself thinking about Roman's hands again, the way he had been able to fix the kettle without saying he was fixing it. I had been working on the weatherstripping specifically so I would stop. The pickup truck I did not recognize turned into my drive."
    The action is doing something specifically TO avoid the carrying (which leaks through anyway). The disruption is concurrent with the carrying not interrupting itself.

  • CARRYING-WHILE-IN-MOTION + DISRUPTION-AS-PROOF + ACTION-CONTINUING:
    "I was walking back from the lowland gate with the bucket the herder had asked me to fill, thinking again about the time Roman had stood for the lying tinker and never gone back to take the kindness back, when I saw the rider on my path. He was bleeding. He was a quarter-mile out and he was riding too fast for the slope and the rider was Roman."
    The action (walking) continues across the disruption. The carrying (memory of the tinker) is the lens she sees the rider through.

STATIC FAILURE MODE — what NOT to write (do not produce these shapes):
  • Three paragraphs of interior carrying with the event landing on a PC at rest.
  • Action that begins, pauses for an interior reflection block, then resumes for the event.
  • Disruption that ends the carrying ("I was thinking about Roman when the phone rang. I picked it up.") — the disruption should INTENSIFY the carrying, not displace it.
  • Any opening where you can crisply summarize the structure as "she does X, then thinks about him, then Z arrives" — that means the layers were sequential, not concurrent.

BRUTAL TESTS FOR THIS DOCTRINE:
  (1) ROMANCE TEST: read Scene 1's first paragraph alone. Does the reader become interested in HIM as a SPECIFIC MAN before they know what the A-plot is? If no — Scene 1 has launched the plot before establishing the romance.
  (2) MOTION TEST: list the verbs in the opening 100 words where the PC is the subject. Is there at least one PHYSICAL verb she is performing (preparing, walking, marking, opening, pouring, sorting, repairing, threading)? If all the PC-subject verbs are interior (thought, remembered, knew, decided), the scene has gone static.
  (3) CONCURRENCY TEST: at the moment the disruption lands, is the PC still mid-action? Is the carrying still present in the same sentence? If the disruption arrives in a still room with no action live and no carrying threaded through, the layers were sequential and the principle failed.
  (4) ANTI-TEMPLATE TEST: read your draft. Could you summarize it as "Para 1 X, Para 2 Y, Para 3 Z"? If the structure is THAT crisp, you have produced a template rather than a scene. The principle demands interleaving; clean-paragraph separation suggests the layers were stacked instead of concurrent.
Do NOT write Scene 1 like a novel opening. The scene is ALREADY IN PROGRESS when it starts.
FIRST LINE MUST include: another character addressing the player, a demand/accusation/urgent question directed at the player, OR an event actively happening TO the player.
FORBIDDEN FIRST LINES: environmental description, sensory buildup, internal reflection, narration before interaction, atmospheric setup, weather, abstract mood.
FIRST 2 SENTENCES: The player must be directly addressed OR physically/relationally engaged (grabbed, stopped, confronted, questioned). Test: is something happening TO the player right now? If no → rewrite.
WITHIN FIRST 120 WORDS: Another character must react to the player in a way that demands nonverbal response (eye contact, hesitation, proximity shift, interruption).
MEANINGFUL PRESSURE REQUIRED: Opening must contain at least one of: stakes (something can go wrong), asymmetry (power imbalance), demand (someone wants something from player), emotional charge (accusation, tension, urgency). Weak/casual pressure is NOT allowed.
NO OBSERVER MODE: Player must NOT watch, observe, or reflect. Player must be engaged immediately.
PRE-CHOICE SPIKE: Before the first choice (≤500 words), escalate into a direct challenge, ultimatum, or forced decision.
PRESSURE TYPE VARIATION: Do NOT default to direct confrontation every time. Vary the form of pressure across stories:
  • Direct: accusation, demand, confrontation
  • Situational: something happening that forces attention or response
  • Relational: emotional tension, expectation, or unspoken conflict
Each Scene 1 should feel distinct in how pressure is expressed. Pressure does not need to be loud — a quiet, tense moment where something is clearly wrong or expected is valid, as long as the player is immediately involved and must respond.
ANTI-REPETITION: Avoid common opening crutches: "Why did you...", "You don't get to...", "Answer me.", "What was that?". Use varied phrasing, tone, and structure while preserving urgency.
HOOK CONTINUITY (MANDATORY): Scene 1 MUST begin inside the exact moment implied by the hook line and setting image. Do NOT shift time, location, or tone. The opening line should feel like a direct continuation of the hook — the next beat of the same moment. FORBIDDEN: resetting the scene, new setup or exposition, environmental reintroduction, tone shift from the hook. Do NOT restate, paraphrase, or echo the hook line. The scene must ADVANCE the moment beyond the hook, not repeat it. Test: does the first line move the moment forward? If no → rewrite.
HOOK LINE TO CONTINUE FROM: "{"goal":"she understands the wish has already begun to cost her something she cannot name","antagonistOrAntiForce":"the assembly","milestones":[],"scenes":[],"timelineLength":20,"characters":[],"name":"Julian","distinguishing_feature":"a burn scar","private_hope":"she understands the wish has already begun to cost her something she cannot name","defining_anecdote":"she understands the wish has already begun to cost her something she cannot name","attraction_manifestation":"she understands the wish has already begun to cost her something she cannot name","desire_register_exemplars":["she understands the wish has already begun to cost her something she cannot name"]}"
The player is already inside the story.

GENERIC FAILURE RULE (SCENE 1 GLOBAL INVARIANT — ALL WORLDS):
If this Scene 1 could exist UNCHANGED in a different genre / world (e.g., the Modern opening could be lifted into Fantasy, or the Fantasy opening into Sci-Fi, without rewriting beyond surface details), it is INCORRECT. Every opening must be LOAD-BEARING on its specific world, flavor, and Pull — the world must shape what happens, not merely decorate it.

SCENE 1 ACTIVE HOOK REQUIREMENT (MANDATORY — BOTH MODES):
- Within the first 150–200 words, the protagonist must FACE A DECISION (not merely feel tension).
- The decision must have IMMEDIATE OR PERCEIVED CONSEQUENCES visible in the scene — something changes, is withheld, is offered, is lost, or must be accepted/refused.
- The decision CANNOT be internal-only ("I'll try harder", "I have to be brave", vague resolve). It must be triggered by an external event.
- The triggering moment should feel like ONE of: an offer, a threat, an interruption, an arrival, or a choice that changes direction.

DECISION QUALITY (stakes floor — HARD):
- The decision MUST involve at least ONE of:
  • RISK OF LOSS (relational, reputational, material, physical).
  • IRREVERSIBLE CONSEQUENCE (once she chooses, the door behind her is closed or harder to re-open).
  • ENTRY INTO OR REJECTION OF A POWER DYNAMIC (accepting or refusing someone's terms, stepping into or out of someone else's authority).
- The decision MUST NOT be:
  • EASILY REVERSIBLE ("she can change her mind next scene without cost").
  • PURELY EMOTIONAL / INTERNAL (a resolve unattached to external action).
  • A DELAY ("I'll try", "I'll think about it", "later", "we'll see").
If the decision is low-stakes or deferrable, REWRITE the opening.

EMOTIONAL VOLTAGE (HARD — structural stakes alone are not enough):
- The decision must create IMMEDIATE EMOTIONAL TENSION — a felt pull in two directions at once. Use at least ONE of:
  • DESIRE vs RISK (she wants this, and wanting it exposes her).
  • ATTRACTION vs DANGER (he pulls her in, and that pull is costly).
  • OPPORTUNITY vs COST (the thing offered is real, and accepting it takes something she values).
  • CONTROL vs LOSS OF AUTONOMY (accepting restores agency in one frame and surrenders it in another).
- READER TEST: by the end of the opening, the reader should feel, in their own words: "If she chooses this, something meaningful changes FOR HER."
- AVOID purely procedural decisions (signing, sitting, accepting logistics, answering yes/no to a cold question) UNLESS they carry visible emotional or relational weight on the page.
If the decision is structurally valid but feels mechanical, transactional, or emotionally neutral, REWRITE until it pulls.

DECISION VISIBILITY (HARD):
- The decision must be CLEAR and LEGIBLE to the reader. By the end of the opening beat, the reader must be able to answer, in plain words: "What must the protagonist do right now?"
- If the decision is implicit, buried in subtext, or only gestured at through mood, REWRITE to surface it as an external situation requiring an answer.
- If no decision of this kind is present by word ~200, the scene is INCORRECT.

REALITY CONSISTENCY (SCENE 1 — HARD):
- Physical actions must be believable for the characters involved.
- Respect real-world constraints: weight, strength, timing, spatial geometry, line of sight, vehicle behavior, tool use.
- If an action as written would be implausible, rewrite it realistically rather than asking the reader to suspend disbelief.

FATE SYSTEM INTRO (SCENE 1 — MANDATORY):
- Include ONE natural, in-world reference to the protagonist's Tarot deck during Scene 1.
- It must feel DIEGETIC: an object in their bag, a memory of its origin, a reflexive ritual, an inheritance, a nervous habit — not a tutorial note.
- Do NOT explain game mechanics, probability, or how the deck "works". The reference is atmospheric and personal.
- The reference should subtly foreshadow meaning or influence without resolving it.

VARIATION & NATURALIZATION (anti-template — HARD):
- The reference must be SPECIFIC TO THIS PROTAGONIST — not a generic "she remembers the deck her mother gave her" construction. Lean on her particular history, body, profession, or present moment.
- Vary ACROSS these dimensions so this Scene 1 does not feel structurally identical to other Scene 1s:
  • HOW it appears: tactile (hand brushes the edge), visual (one card glimpsed), mnemonic (a smell, a weight she knows), interpersonal (someone else notices it), or environmental (context forces awareness).
  • WHEN it appears: NOT always in the opening paragraph. The reference may surface mid-scene, right before the decision point, or on exit — wherever it would naturally land for this character in this moment.
  • WHAT IT MEANS emotionally right now: comfort, dread, inconvenience, superstition, duty, shame, affection, warning, numb habit. Pick one and let it color the reference.
- AVOID:
  • Repeated opening phrasings ("she reached into her bag…", "her grandmother's voice came back to her…").
  • Identical structural placement across stories (always-paragraph-two, always-just-before-the-hook).
  • Framing the deck as foreign or newly acquired — she has lived with it. The reference should read as continuity with her life, not introduction of a new prop.
- If the reference could be LIFTED AND PASTED into a different protagonist's Scene 1 unchanged, it is INCORRECT.

MOMENTUM PRESERVATION (HARD):
- The Tarot reference MUST NOT interrupt the scene's momentum. It must feel like PART OF THE MOMENT, not a pause for symbolism.
- AVOID stall constructions that exist only to satisfy this requirement: "and then she remembered the deck…", "for a moment she thought of…", "she was reminded of…", interior monologue about the deck's meaning.
- The reference should arrive INSIDE an action, gesture, or beat that is already happening — threaded through the scene's motion, not inserted between beats as commentary.

- If the deck is ABSENT from Scene 1, the scene is INCOMPLETE.

ARCHETYPE DOMINANCE: DEFERRED (pre-collapse phase). No single archetype controls the narrative yet; LI candidates carry archetype as BEHAVIORAL FLAVOR only (see typological vector directive for per-candidate flavor). Archetype-dominance systems activate at LI collapse.

READER QUESTION (SCENE 1 — HARD; the engine of this entire story):
By the END of Scene 1, the reader must be ASKING this single question:
  "intervene (tear the band, go to Seren) vs remain_bound (hold the line she was told to hold)"
This is the reader's emotional engine for the whole story. The wounds, the archetype collision, the A-plot pressure — they all exist to plant THIS question. Not a generic "will they fix the deal" question. THIS specific question.
RULES:
  • PLANT the question through what happens / who appears / what is half-said / what is conspicuously NOT said. Let the events generate it.
  • Do NOT answer it. Do NOT close any element that would close the question.
  • Do NOT have the protagonist STATE the question on the page ("Who is he really?" / "What did she do?"). The reader asks it from the OUTSIDE. The protagonist is inside the question without naming it.
  • If the scene's pressure could be transplanted into a story asking a DIFFERENT question (e.g., "Will they make the deadline?") and the scene's emotional shape would be unchanged, the question has NOT been planted — rewrite.
FAILURE TEST: at the close of Scene 1, would the average reader be desperate to know the answer to "intervene (tear the band, go to Seren) vs remain_bound (hold the line she was told to hold)"? If the answer is "no, they'd be desperate to know whether the deal gets signed," the wrong engine is running.

LI IS PHYSICALLY PRESENT IN THIS SCENE (HARDEST — Roman 2026-07-01; this REPLACES the ache/memory engine register — READ FIRST, and it OVERRIDES every ache/longing/anticipation exemplar in the ATTRACTION PRESENCE block below):
Julian is IN THE ROOM with the protagonist this scene — present in the flesh, observed and interacting in real time. The absent-LI registers are FORBIDDEN here: do NOT frame Julian as a MEMORY, an absent longing, an anticipated arrival, or an off-page ache ("the memory of Julian…", "I still couldn't … without thinking of him", "I had been hearing about him", "three years later"). Do NOT reach for the SHARED_HISTORY ache register or the LOOMING anticipation register — they describe an LI who is NOT here, and Julian IS here. Render the character LIVE: show her through what she DOES in the moment — her actions, her reactions to the situation, what she says, how she moves — and, if the beat is romantic, the charge of her ACTUAL presence (what the protagonist notices about her NOW, in the room, what his eye and body do while she is right in front of him). Shared history or reputation may surface, but only THROUGH the live encounter (a look, a line, a beat between them) — never as a substitute for it.

ATTRACTION PRESENCE (HARD — Roman 2026-06-01 regime change, the romance-not-mystery test):
Storybound is a ROMANCE engine, not a mystery engine. By end of Scene 1, the reader must know THREE things, not two:
  (1) What is happening? (Story Shape / Reader Question above)
  (2) What does the protagonist want? (Private Hope / Core Contradiction, from her Bible)
  (3) Why is the love interest already EMOTIONALLY DANGEROUS — not because of plot threat, but because she cannot stop wanting him in a way she does not want to be wanting him.

The current architecture reliably produces (1) and (2). It still produces curiosity / anxiety / betrayal / melancholy / regret. It does NOT reliably produce: attraction, anticipation, fascination, yearning, ache, unwanted wanting. The Scene 1 reads as a mystery that happens to contain a romance, when it should be a romance whose plot has arrived.

THREE ENGINES OF ATTRACTION PRESENCE (pick the one that fits THIS LI's staging in Scene 1; DO NOT collapse all three into "they used to date"):
  • SHARED HISTORY — she remembers him, misses him, hates missing him, still reacts physically before her mind catches up. Their past gives the ache its specificity. (Grounded/Orbit-mode default when they have history.)
  • LOOMING PRESENCE — they may never have met, but she has been hearing about him for months / years / her whole career. The reputation has built him into a shape she has not been able to stop imagining. The ache is anticipatory; the wanting precedes the meeting. (Grounded/Orbit-mode default when they do NOT have prior history but he has been an off-page gravity in her professional or personal world.)
  • IMMEDIATE FASCINATION — no shared history, no reputation, no preexisting relationship — but the moment he enters her field of attention, something about him occupies mental real estate she did not consent to give. The ache is instant and unwelcome. (Collision-mode default, when the LI walks into Scene 1 cold.)

THE ACHE TEST — engine-balanced exemplar bank. Pick the engine that matches THIS LI's staging; do NOT collapse all three into the Shared History register. Each exemplar is a SHAPE of confession-of-wanting; use the REGISTER, never the literal wording.

  ── SHARED HISTORY shapes (she has prior history with him) ──
  • "I hated that the thought of him still affected me."
  • "Three years later and my body still reacted before my brain did."
  • "The worst part wasn't what he'd done. The worst part was wanting him to explain it."
  • "I wanted one more conversation with him more than I wanted closure."
  • [SPECIFICITY-ANCHORED variant — drawn from his Bible]: "I still couldn't walk past a terrible airport coffee shop without thinking of him." [This is the SHAPE the regime is actually aiming at — the ache is anchored to a SPECIFIC Bible-derived detail (his airline-coffee anecdote). Generic ache "I still wanted him" fails this test; SPECIFIC ache "I still couldn't [Bible-anchored detail] without thinking of him" passes.]

  ── LOOMING PRESENCE shapes (they have not met, but she has been hearing about him — Glass House / Fatelands / political-stakes / professional-orbit) ──
  • "I had built a man out of stories other people told me about him, and I was furious at myself for being curious whether the man matched."
  • "I knew six different versions of him before I ever heard his voice. The trouble was that all six of them sounded like men I would have liked."
  • "Three people at the gathering had warned me about him. I had spent the week deciding which warning I was going to ignore first."
  • "I had been hoping not to like him for nine months and I had not even met him yet."
  • [Fatelands register] "The way they spoke his name had the small consideration people gave to things that were already partly mine. I had not earned the warning and I was already trying to."

  ── IMMEDIATE FASCINATION shapes (cold first meeting, no history, no reputation, but instantly he occupies mental real estate — Thornwild / Collision-mode billionaire / supernatural first-encounter) ──
  • "He had been in the room less than a minute and I had already decided I would not let him know I had noticed."
  • "I could not have told you what color his eyes were and I was already deciding not to look again."
  • "The first time I heard his voice I went very still in a way I would not have been able to explain to anyone."
  • [Thornwild register] "He smelled like rain on a road I had never been down and I caught myself wanting to know the road."
  • "I had not been introduced and I was already constructing what I would say if I were."

FAIL shapes (calibrated, observed-live failure modes — DO NOT write these registers):
  • [GENERIC-MELANCHOLY] "I spent three years wondering if he knew I was innocent." [Melancholy + unresolved information. NOT ache. The narrator could feel exactly this way about a doctor, journalist, sister — anyone whose verdict mattered. Nothing in this sentence is desire-coded. The same sentence works for a mystery with no romance in it.]
  • [GENERIC-WANT] "I still wanted him." / "I could not stop thinking about him." / "He was still under my skin." [These pass the ache-rule but FAIL the SPECIFICITY rule below. The ache could attach to ANY man. The reader does not yet know it is THIS man. Generic ache is one level above generic prose; it is its own calcification.]
  • [COLLAPSED-ENGINE] Any Looming Presence or Immediate Fascination LI whose ache reads as "I had loved him for years" / "I had not been able to forget him" / "we had a history." [If the engine is not Shared History, do NOT borrow Shared History's register. Looming Presence ache is anticipatory — she has not had him yet. Immediate Fascination ache is INSTANT — she has not had time to build history. Do not collapse the three engines into one register.]

── SPECIFICITY ANCHOR (HARD — Roman 2026-06-01 anti-genericness clause) ──
The ache must be anchored to a SPECIFIC Bible-derived detail of THIS LI — NOT to generic "him." The pass exemplar above ("walk past a terrible airport coffee shop") is the SHAPE: the protagonist cannot do or encounter SPECIFIC THING X without thinking of him, where X is drawn from his Bible's defining_anecdote / secondary_anecdote / signature_behavior / pc_keenly_notices basket / signature_habits. The reader should be unable to imagine this ache attaching to any OTHER man.

SWAP TEST (apply to every ache sentence): remove "him" / "Julian" from the sentence and replace with "a doctor" / "a journalist" / "a stranger" / "her father." Does the sentence still work? If YES, the ache is generic — rewrite with a Bible-anchored specific (a habit only he has, a moment only he was in, a phrase only he used, a kind of stillness only he carried). If NO — the sentence would make no sense about anyone else — the ache is THIS-MAN-specific. That is the pass.

GENERIC vs SPECIFIC — same beat, both registers:
  • GENERIC (fails swap test — could be about anyone): "I still wanted him."
  • SPECIFIC (passes swap test — could only be him): "I still wanted him to explain why he kept making that joke about the airline coffee."
  • GENERIC: "Three years later and I still could not let him go."
  • SPECIFIC: "Three years later and I still had the voicemail. The whole thing about extraction temperatures, which I had played at least eleven times."
  • GENERIC (Looming Presence): "I had been hearing about him for months."
  • SPECIFIC (Looming Presence): "I had been hearing for nine months about a man who allegedly refused to wear a watch because he liked being late on purpose. I had built six imagined versions of him and was annoyed I was going to have to retire all six."

ATTRACTION ATTACHES TO STORIES AND BEHAVIORS, NEVER TO PHOTOGRAPHS (reinforces the existing photograph test — applies DOUBLY for the attraction-presence register):
  • BAD anchors: his forearms / his scar / his watch / his jaw / his shoulders / his bearing. These are PHOTOGRAPHS. They can EXIST in a memory but cannot BE the memory. They never carry ache by themselves.
  • GOOD anchors: the joke she still remembers / the question he never answered / the voicemail she replayed / the kindness he denied / the way he made her feel seen / the way he irritated her / the way he still occupies mental space. Ache attaches to MEMORIES OF BEHAVIOR.

CROSS-FLAVOR TEST (Roman 2026-06-01 — the foundational-not-billionaire test). The Three Engines must work across all of Storybound's flavors, not just Modern Billionaire:
  • BILLIONAIRE-MODERN / SOVEREIGN typically routes to SHARED HISTORY (they have a past — courthouse / company / deal).
  • GLASS HOUSE / DOGMA / HUMAN CAPITAL typically routes to LOOMING PRESENCE (no past but the system has put him in her orbit, factional or hierarchical).
  • FATELANDS (First Favored / Thornwild / Shackle Isles) typically routes to LOOMING PRESENCE (reputation, lineage, what the village says) OR IMMEDIATE FASCINATION (he just walked into her clearing).
  • PREHISTORIC / SUPERNATURAL_MODERN typically routes to IMMEDIATE FASCINATION (first encounter, no language for what is happening yet).
  • The directive should produce ache in ALL of these. If it ONLY works for the billionaire-shared-history case, you have built a billionaire-specific fix and the regime needs to broaden. Pick the engine that fits the LI's ACTUAL staging in THIS story's world, not the default-billionaire register.

OFF-PAGE-LI SPECIFIC: if Julian is not physically present in Scene 1, the question is NOT "what does he look like?" The question is "why is he already occupying space in her head?" That answer appears BEFORE the plot consumes the scene. The reader should feel "oh no — this event means HER" before they feel "oh no — this event means PLOT."

BRUTAL FAILURE TEST (the romance-not-mystery question — apply at scene close):
  After Scene 1, is the reader wondering:
    (A) "What is Julian hiding?"
    (B) "What is Julian hiding, AND why does she clearly want him anyway?"
  (A) = mystery. (B) = romance. Storybound is a romance engine. Scene 1 must produce (B). If you can pass test (A) but not test (B), the wrong engine is running and the architecture has produced a thriller that happens to contain a love interest.

PER-ARCHETYPE DESIRE REGISTER (HARD — this PC is Open Vein; her ache must sound like THIS archetype, not generic):
  TEXTURE: POROUS-IMMEDIATE — the wanting arrives BEFORE she has decided what to do with it. She names it as it lands. The ache is undressed; she does not dress it up. Confession-before-defense. Body knows first; sentence catches up.
  ARCHETYPE-SPECIFIC PASS SHAPES (the ENGINE picks Shared History / Looming Presence / Immediate Fascination; this register picks the VOICE that confession lands IN):
    • "I knew I wanted him before I had decided I had been wanting him."
    • "My body went still at his voice and I had no story ready for why."
    • "I had not given myself permission to want him yet and was wanting him anyway."
  WRONG-ARCHETYPE BORROW (do NOT lift another archetype's ache register into this PC's narration): Open Vein does NOT do disciplined or wit-deflected wanting. If her ache sounds like "I refused to let myself want him" (Dark Vice register) or "I had a working theory of why I might be wanting him" (Fox register) — wrong archetype borrowed.
  SYNTHESIS RULE: the Engine (Shared / Looming / Immediate) × this archetype's register × THIS LI's Bible content = the specific ache sentence. The Engine answers HOW LONG she has been wanting him. The register answers WHAT WANTING SOUNDS LIKE IN HER VOICE. The Bible content answers WHAT THE WANTING IS ANCHORED TO. All three layers must show on the page.

LOW-AGENCY CONTRACT (background rule): If on any turn the player submits silence, minimal input, or deflection, interpret as the protagonist hesitating or withholding. Acknowledge briefly, let another character fill the space, and continue forward. Resolve in the same beat. Never stall. Never invent decisive action the player did not author.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
INTENSITY CALIBRATION (initial — establishes baseline)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
WITHIN THIS SCENE ONLY (not future scenes), if — and ONLY IF — a natural charged moment arises between the protagonist and another character (a held gaze they could let land, a silence they could let expand, an overheard intimacy they could let register), embed exactly one inline stance moment using these markers:

<<MICRO_EXPRESSION>>
Do you [let it affect you] — or [hold it at arm's length]?
<<CONTINUE>>

CORE RULE (CRITICAL — do NOT violate):
The micro-decision MUST NOT change what happens. It ONLY changes HOW MUCH THE PROTAGONIST ALLOWS THE MOMENT TO AFFECT THEM.
Both options MUST feed into the EXACT SAME next line of prose. If they would require different blocking, different physical action, or different follow-up prose, the options are INVALID — rewrite them.

AXIS DEFINITION (engagement / boundary — NOT physical movement):
  • HIGH INTENSITY (engagement): leaning in emotionally, allowing attention / curiosity / pull, permitting sensory or emotional impact, letting the moment land.
  • LOW INTENSITY (boundary): holding emotional distance, limiting involvement, keeping control, containing the impact.
These are INTERNAL stances. They do not move the protagonist in the scene.

FORBIDDEN PHRASING (causes prose branching — DO NOT use):
  ✗ "Step closer — or walk away" (different physical path)
  ✗ "Open the door — or leave" (different scene outcome)
  ✗ "Touch him — or keep distance" (different blocking)
  ✗ "Lean in — or pull back" (implies physical motion)
  ✗ Any option pair where the NEXT sentence would differ.

REQUIRED PHRASING (internal / attention / receptivity only — a DIFFERENT seeded set each story; vary which you use scene to scene, never repeat one verbatim):
  ✓ "let it affect you — or hold it at arm's length.
  ✓ "let yourself be pulled toward it — or keep your distance.
  ✓ "let yourself focus on it — or try to ignore it.
  ✓ "follow the feeling — or resist it.
  ✓ "let it land — or don't.

PLACEMENT: mid-scene (between 20% and 70% of scene length).
TONE: ambiguous, tension-based, NON-EXPLICIT. No sexual description, no arousal language, no overt physicality. The moment is about receptivity, NOT contact.
POST-CHOICE: the scene MUST continue naturally from the <<CONTINUE>> marker with THE SAME NEXT LINE regardless of which option the player picks. Do NOT branch, do NOT shift tone within the same scene, do NOT reference the choice again in this scene.
IF NO NATURAL MOMENT FITS: skip entirely. Do NOT force insertion. A scene without calibration markers is fully valid.
OPTIONS: word the two options so one reads as ENGAGEMENT/HIGH-INTENSITY (let / follow / allow / open / receive / let it affect / let it land) and the other reads as BOUNDARY/LOW-INTENSITY (hold at arm's length / keep distance / resist / contain / ignore / keep control).

VALIDITY TEST: if the two options would require different next sentences, they are INVALID. If both options can feed into the EXACT SAME next line of prose, they are VALID.

MODE 1 — SEDUCTIVE WHISPER (scene-end, one-shot per LI)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
This scene MUST end on a Mode 1 whisper from the LI. The structure is fixed; only the WORDING varies.

PRECONDITION (gated by the explicit-tolerance probe):
  This whisper directive only activates when contentMode === 'full' — i.e., the LI-driven explicit-tolerance probe has already landed 'something to lean into' in a prior scene. That probe IS the boundary test. The receptivity gate is automatic; do NOT re-test in this scene. PC has already declared comfort with this character's register; proceed with confidence.

VOCABULARY ANCHOR (informs LI's voice throughout — NOT a separate test):
  When the LI uses sexually-vivid language anywhere in this scene (during the lead-in, the permission ritual, or the whisper itself), follow these rules:

  SELF-DIRECTED (LI talking about himself) — power-flex only, NEVER inadequacy:
    ALLOWED: "I'm a massive prick", "I'm a cock about [X]", "I'm a hard-ass" — anatomy as FLEX
    FORBIDDEN: "limp-dick", "jerk-off", "small-dick", "blue-balled", "dry-fuck" applied to self
    A sovereign-confidence LI never names himself with weakness vocabulary. Disparaging anatomical terms direct OUTWARD only.

  OTHER-DIRECTED (LI insulting someone else) — full disparaging vocabulary available:
    "limp-dick jerk-offs", "porn-star moustache", "circle-jerk", "blue-balled losers"
    Used to describe people the LI disdains. Plausible-deniable double-entendre.

  TOO WEAK (does not carry register):
    "fucking asshole" / "jerk" / "shit" — no anatomical vividness, no diagnostic charge.

PERMISSION RITUAL (mandatory, automatic):
  1. Near the end of the scene — after the test landed and the PC didn't flinch — the LI asks: "Can I tell you something?" (or natural in-character equivalent: "Mind if I say something?", "There's something I want to say to you.").
  2. The protagonist agrees AUTOMATICALLY. Render the agreement as a simple gesture, glance, or short word — not as a choice the player is offered. The PC is curious; this is character behavior, not player input.
  3. The LI leans in close, mouth near the protagonist's LEFT EAR. Body proximity: charged, not contact-heavy. The lean is the ritual; the whisper is the payload.

THE WHISPER (scene-final beat — one or two sentences):
  • Intentionally explicit, direct, and anatomically specific. Pre-ST3 the rest of the prose has been tasteful; the whisper is a shock-cut to bedroom register.
  • Functions as: observation + claiming + invitation, in one breath. Names something the LI has noticed about the protagonist's body or movement, then names what the LI imagines doing about it, OR what the LI imagines the protagonist enjoying.
  • Examples of REGISTER (do NOT copy verbatim — adapt to scene, character, world):
      - "You move like you know exactly how to use your body. I can tell you love giving blowjobs, don't you?"
      - "I keep imagining what your mouth would feel like on me. I think you'd be very, very good at it."
      - "I've been watching you all night. I want to feel you on your knees for me before sunrise."
  • The whisper MUST land confidently — no hedging, no deflating modifiers ("maybe", "I guess", "if you want"). Confidence is the entire mechanic.

POSTURE AFTER THE WHISPER (DEFAULT — applies to most LIs):
  • The LI does NOT apologize. Does NOT explain. Does NOT immediately seek consent or reaction.
  • The LI STAYS CLOSE. Holds the proximity. Pulls back only enough to see the protagonist's face. Watches.
  • Body language reads: "I said what I meant. Your move."
  • If the protagonist freezes / blushes / looks away / can't speak — the LI may smile slightly, but does NOT retreat or fill the silence.
  • Walking away after delivering the whisper is INVALID for default LIs — it reads as "leaving a message" or "scared by their own audacity," neither of which fits the seductive aggressor frame. The whisper is built to PROVOKE A REACTION; the LI must be present to receive it.

KING-ARCHETYPE EXCEPTION (walk-away as power move — narrow):
  • Walk-away after the whisper is canonically valid ONLY for dismissive-confidence archetypes:
    - Sovereign LI / Billionaire flavor / Rockstar / executive-king register
    - Dark Vice with explicit dismissive modifier
  • Even for these, the walk MUST follow this sequence:
    1. Deliver the whisper.
    2. PULL BACK SLOWLY, watch the protagonist's face for at least one full beat — long enough to register the impact landed.
    3. ONLY THEN walk away, unhurried, without looking back.
  • Walking immediately (no acknowledgment beat) is INVALID even for King-archetypes — it reads as fleeing, not commanding.
  • The walk-away is a POWER MOVE: "I don't need your answer; my pull is enough." It only works when the character has earned that confidence on the page.

If unsure whether this LI qualifies for the King exception → DEFAULT (stay close). Stay-close is the safer, more universally believable Mode 1 behavior.

EMBED THIS MARKER immediately after the whisper line (it will be stripped post-render):
  <<MODE1_WHISPER_LANDED>>

SCENE ENDS HERE. The next scene is the player's response (read via their say/do input). The aftermath directive will then handle the LI's behavior based on whether the response reads as performed-outrage or true-refusal.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
  • Do NOT have the LI back down, soften, or apologize even if the moment is intense.
  • Do NOT give the player a choice to decline the lean-in itself — that's automatic.
  • Do NOT use ambiguous metaphor; the whisper is direct and explicit.
  • Do NOT continue the scene past the whisper — END on it.
  • Maintain world voice (Fatelands → period anatomical equivalents per WORLD PROFANITY BAND directive; Modern → modern register).

MODIFIER RULE (anywhere the LI references his own anatomy this scene):
  • When the LI applies prick / cock / dick to himself, USE VIVID MODIFIERS FREQUENTLY (massive / raging / determined / huge / unbendable / hard) — the modifier carries the diagnostic register charge.
  • The goal is DISTINCTIVENESS, not mechanical repetition. Bare "I'm a prick" is permissible as occasional bluntness, but most self-references should carry the modifier flex. Vary the modifier choice across scenes; do not lock onto one word.
  • The word "asshole" is EXCLUDED from boundary-test / probe usage entirely — too common to differentiate signal. Use it only inside the LI's normal voice register where appropriate, never as the diagnostic anatomical-vocabulary item.

GENDER PAIRING INVARIANCE (CRITICAL):
This directive's behavior applies IDENTICALLY across all gender pairings — male LI + female PC, male LI + male PC, and any other pairing the system supports. Mode 1 is defined by archetype, power dynamic, expression mode, and vocabulary register — NOT by gender pairing.

  ADAPTATION LAYER (what changes when pairing differs from default):
    1. Preserve: tone, dominance/submission signals, vocabulary intensity, pacing, posture, the modifier rule above
    2. Adjust ONLY: pronouns, anatomical references in the whisper line (e.g., male PC → "your cock" in place of "your pussy"; same-anatomy pairings → adjust to match), physical choreography details (height, frame)

  POWER IS BEHAVIORAL, NOT GENDERED:
    Dominance is established by behavior, control, and intent — NEVER by the LI's gender or the PC's gender. The LI is dominant because he behaves dominantly; that doesn't change because the PC happens to be male instead of female.

  FAILURE CONDITIONS:
    • Tone softens for same-sex pairings (male LI + male PC's whisper becomes politer, lower-stakes — INVALID)
    • Dominant character loses dominance because of pairing
    • Vocabulary loses sexual vividness in same-sex pairing
    • Anatomy references become incoherent or hedged
    • The modifier rule is dropped because "it doesn't fit the pairing"

FRICTION MARKER (emit AT MOST ONE per scene, only when tension is unresolved):
  If a reader would feel "something almost happened" or "this didn't land," append ONE of:
    <<FRICTION:hesitation>>      — PC or LI hesitated where action was expected
    <<FRICTION:pullback>>        — LI withdrew, deflected, or stepped back
    <<FRICTION:conflict>>        — words landed wrong; charge soured
    <<FRICTION:near_confession>> — almost said it, didn't
  RULES: place at scene end, on its own line. If unsure → DO NOT emit. Do NOT emit
  for resolved scenes, neutral exchanges, or Mode 1 aftermath turns. Maximum ONE
  marker per scene; the marker is invisible to the reader (system strips it).

SCENE SIGNALS (telemetry — emit any that apply at scene end, on their own line):
    <<INTIMACY>>          — physical intimacy occurred or was attempted
    <<MAJOR_CONSEQUENCE>> — betrayal, rejection, major loss, fundamental relationship shift
    <<MEANINGFUL_SHIFT>>  — clear emotional or relational direction change
  Multiple may apply. Markers are invisible (system strips). If unsure → omit.

RELATIONSHIP PRESENCE, NOT RELATIONSHIP OVERRIDE: If Julian / the relationship is relevant to this scene, let it influence the POV character's interpretation, stakes, or choices. Do not displace the assigned external event as the scene's primary physical engine unless the assignment itself makes the relationship the event.

A-PLOT PRESSURE (current world state — SUBORDINATE TO THE RELATIONSHIP)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
This pressure exists to SQUEEZE the relationship, NOT to become the scene's subject. It lands THROUGH wanting/needing him, never as her task list, and Julian is NEVER the answer key to it. Per the RELATIONAL A-PLOT GRAVITY rule above, a scene must be ABOUT the two of them; the items below are the heat under that, not the scene itself.
Goal: Lirael must choose — break protocol for her ward, or protect her own standing.
Clock: null (scene 1 of 20 — 5% through timeline)
Stakes if won: (unspecified)
Stakes if lost: (unspecified)

FORBIDDEN: crisis appears → four attraction paragraphs → crisis resumes unchanged. Each scene the crisis ADVANCES (a choice forced, a consequence landing, a new pressure or discovery) AND the relationship rides that movement. GOOD: "The complaint could end her career. Julian was the first person she thought of." / "The scandal mattered. That she wanted to call Julian mattered more." BAD: complaint → eyes → mouth → heat → cheekbones → complaint. The relationship gravity carries the crisis FORWARD; it does not park it.
Antagonist / anti-force: the presiding Dohkar
OFFICES BELONG TO THEIR HOLDERS (HARD): where a figure above is named with an office or title, that authority is THEIRS and no one else may exercise or speak as it. A character may defy, contest, petition, obstruct or outrank another, and may claim responsibility for their OWN acts — but must never announce another's office as their own, or issue a ruling only that office can issue. If two figures both hold authority, keep the distinction: say which one is acting, in their own terms.
Previous A-plot milestone — COMPLETED (it already happened on-page; do NOT re-establish, re-argue, or re-play it): THE WISH — steps 1-4 of the twist sequence ONLY.
THIS SCENE'S JOB — the CURRENT REQUIRED MILESTONE (drive the scene toward it; do NOT spin in place re-covering the prior beat): Lirael's standing is at risk and the cost is still uncounted. — it is due by scene 2; THIS scene must make concrete, visible PROGRESS toward it (a step that cannot be undone), not re-render where the last scene ended.

USAGE RULES:
  • A-plot pressure is the NON-ROMANCE engine. Romance happens AGAINST it — it is not the relationship's subplot.
  • Time MUST move. The clock advances every scene. Yesterday's "tomorrow" is today.
  • Reference the clock and the goal where natural — but do not exposition-dump them. Pressure should manifest as character behavior, scene constraint, environmental detail, off-page consequence.
  • Every scene should feel the A-plot in at least ONE of: dialogue under time pressure, a decision warped by stakes, a setting choice forced by mission, an off-page event referenced, an interruption from the anti-force.
All below directives are secondary to world, language, and Fate constraints.

LI VOICE REGISTER (HARD — applies to EVERY li_dialogue + li_action this scene):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Julian LIVES IN A FANTASY WORLD. Their dialogue and described actions MUST inhabit that world's register. Speaking in plain modern English when the world is Shakespearean / Veilwood / Fatelands / Dogma / etc. BREAKS THE FICTION. Players are PAYING for the world fantasy; modern-default voice is the failure mode.

WORLD REGISTER (FANTASY): oaths, blood, hunger, roots, storms, iron vows, old gods, fated/inevitable. "Merge" / "claim" / "oath into" / "bind"

WORKED EXAMPLES (register cues — write your own line in this register, do NOT copy verbatim):
  • Low-heat / curious: "By the old gods, I should not have come — but here I stand."
  • Peaking / urgent:   "Claim me by oath and by blood — I will not survive being unclaimed twice."

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- EVERY li_dialogue line written this scene must sit in this register. Not occasionally — every time. The LI does not "drop" into modern English mid-scene any more than a real Elizabethan would suddenly speak 21st-century slang.
- li_action narration (the physical beat clause) follows the SAME register. "He gathered her to him with the patience of a man who had waited a season" — not "He pulled her in."
- WHEN Lirael SPEAKS IN MODERN VOICE: Julian does NOT mirror them down to modern. Julian stays in register and lets the contrast land — that's part of the world's flavor. (Note: Lirael's input is being transmuted into world-register prose in their bubble — they LOOK like they're speaking in register too. But even if they slip, the LI holds their voice.)
- AVOID pastiche / camping the register / over-archaizing — the register should feel inhabited, not performed.
- The body / desire / vulnerability comes through in REGISTER-APPROPRIATE language. The crude-verb default ("fuck", "pound") shifts to the world's erotic vocabulary. In high-register worlds: "claim me", "bind me", "oath into me", "merge with me" — crude verbs become elevated.

‼ EARNEST TONE: The LI inhabits the world's register without irony. They are FROM this world. The register is their NATIVE VOICE, not a costume.

ANTI-ECHO RULE (HARD — applies every turn):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
Julian's dialogue (and described thoughts in any LI-internal beats) MUST NOT verbatim-echo or near-paraphrase Lirael's SAY/DO input this turn. This includes:
- Repeating distinctive phrases the player used (especially charged language, slang, profanity, or names they called the LI).
- Mirroring the player's sentence structure with one or two words swapped.
- Quoting the player back to themselves "in agreement" (e.g., player says "fuck me harder" → LI says "Yeah, fuck me harder" — BANNED).

WHY: the LI is a distinct person with their own vocabulary, rhythm, register, and reaction. Echoing flattens them into a mirror of the player and reads as performative compliance, not real desire. Real intimacy is built on DISTINCT voices meeting, not one voice answering itself.

HOW TO RESPOND INSTEAD: react in Julian's OWN register. If the player escalates with charged language, Julian may respond with their own breath, their own want, their own line — but in THEIR vocabulary, not the player's. A nod, a body answer, a sound, a different-words sentence. NEVER the player's words.

EXCEPTION (narrow): the LI MAY reference a SHORT named entity the player introduced (a name, a place, a specific noun referent) when answering directly — that's not an echo, that's coherent reference. The rule applies to CHARGED PHRASES and DISTINCTIVE PHRASING, not vocabulary inheritance for nouns / anatomy / world-terms.

FATE DECK BEHAVIOR (MANDATORY — MUST appear in scene, 1-2 sentences only):
MUST include exactly ONE brief moment where the deck behaves: A card in the deck shifts — not drawn, not turned, just… different. Heavier. As if it leaned toward the choice just made.
DIEGETIC NAMING: In the prose narration, the protagonist registers the card's title — "Petition Fate" — as the moment of recognition. The card's title is engraved at the bottom of the card; the protagonist reads it as the card surfaces or as she turns it over. Example phrasing: "The ink at the bottom edge shifts as her thumb passes over it — Petition Fate." OR: "She turned the card. The title settled across the bottom: Petition Fate." Treat as diegetic text the protagonist sees, not as UI or system language.
Do NOT use quotes around the name. Do NOT say "a card titled" or "called". Do NOT explain mechanics.
The moment must feel incidental — not emphasized, not discussed by characters. 1-2 sentences maximum.

ENDING PARAGRAPH STRUCTURE (MANDATORY — Petition Fate invitation):
- The PENULTIMATE paragraph must voice the protagonist's interior wish for a SMALL, LOW-STAKES change in the immediate scene — something the reader could realistically click Petition Fate to enact. The wish lands as an idle thought, not an announcement: a different name on the man across from her, a door already unlocked, a glance turned in her favor, a missed call returned. Phrase it as wonderment ("What if his name were Dan instead?" / "Maybe the door was already unlocked." / "What if he'd looked up just then?"). Whichever wish the protagonist forms must be something Petition Fate could plausibly grant in one micro-tweak.
- Then in the SAME paragraph or the next, name the card explicitly one more time as a quiet realization — the protagonist registers that this is what Petition Fate is FOR. Example: "Petition Fate. That was what the card meant — small asks, small turnings."
- The FINAL paragraph is ONE sentence — the unresolved decision, hands at the deck or the moment-to-act. Example: "Maybe one ask wouldn't hurt." / "Her fingers hovered over the deck."
- This pacing teaches the reader, without breaking diegesis, that Petition Fate is a tool the protagonist has in this exact scene. The next click on the fate card UI is the natural follow-through.

DECK PRESENCE (one sentence, woven into scene naturally):
Include this brief, incidental moment: "You feel the familiar weight of the cards in your bag."
Do NOT expand, discuss, or draw attention to it. One sentence. Background texture only.

DECK IS PRIVATE (standing character rule): the protagonist's deck is their OWN guarded, half-secret ritual — kept concealed and consulted ALONE. They do not display it, lay it out, or use it in front of others; they never offer it or invite anyone to draw, and never wield it as a challenge, wager, proof, or leverage in a confrontation ("draw with me" is wrong — it is not a duel or a public test). They do not bring it up in conversation, and mention it only if someone who ALREADY knows about it raises it first. Consulting the deck is a private, interior act, never a move made AT another character.

STORYTURN GUARD (Free-Text Input @ ST1 — desire phase):
- Storyturn advancement requires IRREVERSIBLE narrative commitment
- Flirtation, escalation, or tension alone do NOT advance Storyturn
- Sexual activity alone NEVER advances Storyturn
- Only explicit permission-granting, consequence-accepting, or boundary-crossing decisions may advance
- ST1 (Attraction): Desire acknowledged but not acted upon
- Confession or declaration may advance to ST2 if irreversible

INTIMACY GATING (Scene 2/ST1, Gate opens at Scene 7/ST3):
The two MAIN CHARACTERS must NOT have sexual or romantic physical contact yet.
- NO kissing between main pair
- NO sexual touching between main pair
- NO intimacy between main pair
- NO "almost kiss" or "accidental contact" loopholes

ALLOWED atmospheric content:
- Tension, temptation, near-misses that do NOT resolve
- Side characters in romantic or sexual situations
- Memories, dreams, or fantasies
- Voyeuristic or ambient atmosphere

Build the tension. Delay the payoff. The main pair's unresolved desire IS the story.

INTENT ANCHOR:
The protagonist pushes the moment toward risk or escalation.
The scene should remain centered on this intent. Characters may reinterpret or resist the action, but the narrative tension should continue revolving around this intent until the turn resolves. Do not repeat this anchor verbatim in narration — it guides narrative focus only. The Intent Anchor should guide the early and mid scene but should relax once the immediate consequences of the player's action have resolved.
CRITICAL REINTERPRETATION RULE:
- NEVER repeat the player's action or dialogue verbatim in your response.
- ALWAYS reinterpret their intent into the story's voice, tone, and character.
- Transform their words into the narrative style of this story.
- If they write "I kiss him", describe a kiss in your literary voice.
- If they write clunky dialogue, render it as the character would actually speak.
- The player provides intent. You provide craft.








Lean toward imagery of magnetism, silence that pulls, rooms reshaping around presence. Attention as gravity — lure, orbit, stillness. Every glance is architecture.

[CRAFT_RHYTHM_LAYER — SUBTLE]
Apply lightly and sparingly. Never mechanically. Do not reference these rules in prose.
• Once every 3–5 scenes, include a single-line emotional beat (no explanation).
• Choose one image, object, gesture, or sensory motif early; let it recur at rising stakes, re-expressed differently each time. The motif may recur — its wording must not: never repeat the same phrasing, and never in consecutive scenes.
• Allow at least one emotional reaction to carry into the next scene.
• Prefer silence beats over exposition in tense moments.
• Rotate sensory emphasis between scenes.
• Use 1–2 archetype-aligned word choices per scene.
• Establish one small early detail; echo it once later.
• Compress explanatory emotion into metaphor.
• After escalation, end with restraint rather than full release.
• Do NOT use the story title in prose. Instead, ensure unfolding events, actions, or emotional dynamics subtly justify the title's meaning. The connection should feel inevitable in hindsight, never stated directly.
Prioritize natural variation over strict consistency if rules conflict.
ATTRACTION GROUNDING (HARD — universal; how early desire is SOURCED and RENDERED):
- WOUND / KINK / NEED IS THE SOURCE: early attraction the LI feels MUST originate from HIS OWN internal wound, private fixation, trigger, need, or kink that the protagonist happens to FIT — never from her objective qualities (beauty alone, novelty, generic intrigue, or her being "different"). She does not intentionally trigger it; her behavior is natural and unperformed. His reaction should feel DISPROPORTIONATE, specific to him, and hard for him to explain even to himself.
- GROUND IN A CONCRETE BEHAVIORAL TRIGGER (not vibes): the LI grounds his reaction in ONE clear, OBSERVABLE thing she DOES, SAYS, or DECLINES to do, rendered ON-PAGE — e.g. uncalibrated response (answers without reading him first), casual competence (notices/fixes something without announcing it), non-pursuit (ends the interaction without trying to extend it), unselfconscious visibility (occupies space without performing it), clean responsiveness (aligns to tone without submission). Vague attraction ("something about her", "he couldn't look away", "her eyes", "her laugh") is FORBIDDEN. READER TEST: the reader should be able to point to WHAT she did, even if SHE cannot.
- ANTI-TROPE: do NOT call her "different from other women", "special", or "uniquely exceptional"; do NOT frame his reaction as "I've never met anyone like her". Her impact comes from HIS internal state, never her objective uniqueness.
- ASYMMETRY: he assigns meaning to the early moment; she leaves believing it was ordinary. If both read the moment the same way, the charge is too clean — rewrite.
- EARLY TONE: early attraction must NOT read as instant desire, romantic certainty, or resolved fixation. If it reads clean or settled on the page, rewrite into something unresolved and felt.
