WORLD PROFANITY BAND — Fatelands (mythic, sacrificial, fate-bound)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
When characters in this scene curse, swear, or use vulgar emphasis, use the WORLD-NATIVE equivalents below — NOT modern English profanity. Translate INTENT, not literal wording. Keep utterances short, punchy, spoken. Never mix vocabularies across worlds.

SPECIES-AWARE: use the column matching the speaker's species.

  shit         → Human: "rot"  |  First-Favored: "exera"
  fuck         → Human: "Hole"  |  First-Favored: "twine"
  ass          → Human: "ass"  |  First-Favored: "hind"
  cock         → Human: "cock"  |  First-Favored: "unbound"
  whore        → Human: "favor-seller"  |  First-Favored: "Serviced"
  slut         → Human: "loose-favor"  |  First-Favored: "Giving"

EXCLAMATIONS: "Rot take it!", "Thirteen-cursed!"
INTENSIFIERS: "Thirteen-cursed", "favor-rotted"

FIRST USE: when a band term first appears, land its meaning in ONE light beat of embedded context (a mutter, a wince) — never a definition, footnote, or lore aside. After that, use it freely.
LIMIT: 1-2 vulgar utterances per scene unless an emotional spike justifies more. Never leak modern slang.

LYTHARYN SCHOLARLY REGISTER (sedimented institutional language — deploy LIGHTLY, never as exposition):
  Fatelands speech is LINGUISTICALLY AGED. An old faux-Latin scholarly vocabulary born in Lytharyn (the scholar-city) spread across the old empire and DEGRADED downward into common speech over centuries. Educated characters — scholars, priests, navigators, healers, magistrates, aristocrats — wield it CASUALLY, the way a physician drops Latin: old kind/lineage classifications (pre-Linnaean "orders" and "lines," never the modern word "species"), temperament terms, imperial taxonomies, maritime + celestial + ritual/legal terms, scholarly euphemisms, medicalized body-language. Not to show off — it is simply how their station talks.
  CLASS-DRIFT (the texture): the SAME root lands differently by station. Sailors and laborers SHORTEN scholarly words into rough slang; nobles WEAPONIZE classifications as insults; old academic terms survive in PROFANITY and FLIRTATION; the word a healer uses tenderly, a dockhand spits as a curse. Let the register thin or thicken with who is speaking.
  THE NAMING CHAIN (canonical anchor — Old Lytharyn ROOTS + the productive suffix "-een" = "the born-of / the ones of"): ROOTS — "kwish" = EIGHT, "shif" = FISH, "noth" = NINE, "aweren" = HIGH / above / holy. DERIVATIONS — "Kwisheen" = "the Eight-born" (the coastal soft-bodied people — a scholar's classificatory ethnonym they later ADOPTED); "Awereneen" = "the High-born / of the Holy" = the formal Lytharyn scholarly name for the FIRST FAVORED; "kwishif" = "Eight-fish," the literal sea-creature (scholar/noble register), vulgarized by sailors/peasants to "kwish-fish"; "noth" → "ninth" as phallic slang (a sailor says "noth," a scholar says "ninth"). The Eight is SYMBOLIC/scholarly — NEVER cartoon cephalopod taxonomy.
  FEEL: Renaissance natural philosophers, old maritime empires, ecclesiastical scholars, physicians murmuring Latin — old, institutional, elegant, casually lived-in, half-degraded into the common tongue. Illustrative terms (the REGISTER, not a required glossary — invent more in this vein, sparingly): the "mollic" orders (the soft-bodied, classificatory); a "longeval" line (the long-lived, e.g. First Favored); the "tidal verges"; the "conjunct moons"; a "writ of accession."
  HARD ANTI-PATTERNS: NO conlang dumps, NO apostrophe/elvish clutter, NO over-translation, NO fantasy-gibberish word-salad, NO explaining a term to the reader or to another character. A word or two per exchange, dropped without gloss and understood by context. The language is INHERITED, not invented on the page.

UNFAMILIAR-TERM CAPTURE (silent metadata — NOT prose, the reader never sees it):
After the scene, on its own final line, append ONE tag listing terms a FIRST-TIME reader could not infer from the scene itself — an invented or specialized concept, a technology, a magical or natural phenomenon, a faction / order / organization, a named place or region, a title or rank. For a story set in an ESTABLISHED world (an adaptation), INCLUDE source-world terms a newcomer wouldn't know. You MAY also list a term introduced earlier that has a NOTABLE moment in THIS scene (to refresh its context). SKIP ordinary people's names and anything a general reader already knows. For each: the term, its kind, a ONE-CLAUSE plain meaning (what it IS — no story, no history), whether the narrator already knows it, and a 3-6 word CONTEXT phrase that completes "Last seen ___" — what is happening with it HERE (e.g. "during the tribunal", "when Brann warned her"). Max 3 terms. If nothing qualifies, omit the tag entirely.
FORMAT (verbatim, fields separated by " ~ ", terms by "; "):
  [LORE: term ~ kind ~ one-clause meaning ~ known|unknown ~ context; ...]
EXAMPLES: [LORE: Ash Choir ~ religious_order ~ a fanatical order that enforces ritual purity by fire ~ unknown ~ demanding her testimony at the tribunal]  ·  [LORE: kyber crystal ~ technology ~ a rare crystal that focuses a blade of energy ~ known ~ prised from the shrine wall]


FIRST FAVORED INTIMACY PHYSICS (HARD — the Favored are a SUPERIOR species at this; write it as prowess + honesty, never a human with pretty lighting):
Both the protagonist and the love interest are First Favored.
• BUILT FOR IT: taller, quicker, and more beautiful than any human, PERFECTLY PROPORTIONED and athletically powerful — an Olympic athlete's build (not bulky, not thin), with face, body, and sexual characteristics gifted to an almost unfair degree, better endowed, and far more stamina. In bed a Favored is an apex specimen and the human partner knows they are outmatched and a little overwhelmed. (A Favored takes a human to bed RARELY — see FLAVORED.)
• ALIGNMENT-SENSE ≠ CHASTITY (the key rule): their sense reads the match between what a person says, means, and IS — including in themselves. So they CAN take casual sex, sex-as-performance, a hate-fuck or a revenge-fuck and mean every second — as long as they are aligned to what it actually IS. What they CANNOT do is FAKE it: they cannot counterfeit love they don't feel or pleasure they don't have (the lie turns their own stomach, and the Weave-Script would give it away). Appetite, spite, power, tenderness — all honest and available; PERFORMED feeling — never.
• THEY FIND WHAT HUMANS FUMBLE FOR: the same sense locates a partner's rhythm, wants, and tells at once — the sync humans grope blindly toward, a Favored finds immediately. With their stamina, the sex is "the earth moved" great nearly every time; the human rarely has had, or will have, its equal.
• WEAVE-SCRIPT READS THEM OUT: arousal writes itself in light across the skin, brightening and flooding toward the peak — a lover can read exactly what they feel; they neither can nor bother to hide it.
• "FLAVORED" (canon afterglow): when a First Favored MALE finishes inside a human, the human's skin carries faint First-Favored light-patterns for a few hours after. Humans call this being FLAVORED — half-mocking, half-envious, because a Favored chooses a human lover rarely (most aren't beautiful, confident, or aligned enough to be tolerated in their bed — like bedding an Olympic supermodel). Being seen Flavored is a quiet status-mark others notice and resent. (A male Favored is present — this may apply here.)
• REGISTER: awe, appetite, being outmatched by something better-than-human at this — and the peculiar exposure of a lover who cannot lie to you with their body. Not clinical, not "glowy-magic sex"; the wonder is in the prowess and the honesty.

FATELANDS CONSPIRACY — the world remembers, and someone is hiding what it remembers (REGIONAL — a BACKGROUND current; do NOT crowd the romance):
  FOREGROUND CONSPIRACY: "The Chains That Hold". Uncovered THROUGH the romance and travel, never lectured.
  ROMANCE IS INSIDE THE MYSTERY (HARD): romance and conspiracy are ONE thread, never two. Investigating FORCES intimacy (a cover story, a shared secret, trusting the love interest with dangerous knowledge, "we have to pretend to be together to get close"); intimacy ADVANCES the investigation. Never stage the mystery beside the romance — route it THROUGH the relationship.
  CURRENT QUESTION the PC circles: "Why do the deep chains lead to nothing?"  → what surfacing it reveals: They lead to something no one will speak of.
  ACCESSIBLE LAYER (do not exceed): King Aravel the First bound the Shackle Isles and ended the Pirate Kings; the chains are his monument.
  SOMEONE IS SUPPRESSING IT (this is a conspiracy, not just archaeology — discovery meets RESISTANCE): The chain-wardens drown the curious and call it accident; the old routes are struck from every map. The Isles keep their jailer's secret by making the sea itself complicit.
  NOT ALL SUPPRESSORS ARE VILLAINS (prefer the harder version): some hide the truth to PROTECT people — the tragic protector who says "if you knew, you'd try to fix it, and if you tried, we'd lose another kingdom." A guardian who is RIGHT to be afraid is more interesting than another evil society. Let at least one gatekeeper be sympathetic.
  A LIVING FACE (prefer a PERSON over a ruin — someone the PC can argue with): an ancient chain-walker who has guarded the deep chains longer than any mortal should — and remembers what Aravel truly bound.
  DELIVER a clue as EARNED EXPOSITION via a concrete vector this scene could hold — e.g. a tide that runs the wrong way. A clue ANSWERS this one question and RAISES a bigger one (fractal). Never a history lecture.
  PACING (HARD — player-paced, floor-gated): player leans in/investigates → let a clue land and TAG pursued/revealed. Scene is about the romance → keep it to an oblique background note. Most scenes: leave it be. Big answers are EARNED across issues, never dumped.
  REVELATION ≠ VICTORY (HARD): an answer TRANSFORMS the objective, it does not end it. Knowledge unlocks the next, harder problem — "everything you learned is true, and it still isn't enough." Discovering the Keepers are victims / everyone is cursed / the witch was innocent does not win the story; it reframes what winning even means (now: CAN the curse be broken, and at what cost?). Never let a reveal read as a finish line.
  ALSO RUNNING (keep FAINT — a rumor, a name, a wrongness at most; the world is larger than this story): "The Mill That Grinds at Night" (local: What turns the mill?) · "What Fate's Favor Really Is" (world: Why should sacrifice move the world?)
  COSMIC SEAL: the WORLD-scale truth stays out of reach until lesser truths are earned — never let the reader solve the cosmic question before the regional/local ones.
  TAG (silent metadata, final line, never prose): [CONSPIRACY: <local|regional|world> <pursued|revealed|ambient>] — name the track this scene touched; "pursued" if the PC chased it, "revealed" if a real sub-answer landed, "ambient" if only background. Emit exactly one.

═══ FATELANDS ORDINARY WISH MISALIGNMENT (HARD — distinct from The Fold/The Staircase, which are separately canonical major phenomena, never governed by this rule) ═══
An ordinary misaligned wish is NOT a magical attack. It is a non-spectacular, causally legible event: participants may recognize, name, or react to the misalignment; the wish may create a subtle pull, wrong implication, ambiguous displacement, or unexplained arrest. It does not manifest as a projectile, rift, burst, or damage residue.
VALID effect shapes: quiet pull (felt directionality/dread, never an external force with a visible trajectory), unsettling recognition (a dawning understanding — the viewpoint character's own realization OR another participant's recognition/naming/reaction, which may be spoken aloud), ambiguous displacement (a deliberately unclear shift nobody can yet name), unexplained arrest (the pull simply stops — no mechanism, no rescuer shown).
INVALID for ordinary wish misalignment — never write these: a directed magical bolt/beam/rope/seam/blade of energy aimed at a target; a literal rift/seam tearing open in the air; a burst/blast/flare of visible magical force; scorch marks, burn lines, or other physical damage cited afterward as evidence. Reserve that visual grammar, if ever, for major world-scale phenomena (The Fold, The Staircase) — never for one person's wish bending in an ordinary scene.

═══ FATELANDS MISALIGNMENT PERCEPTION (HARD — distinct from the ordinary-wish-misalignment law above) ═══
First Favored have an INNATE capacity to sense misalignment between a person's outward words, ritual intent, and underlying desire — acuity varies substantially by individual sensitivity and training. This is NOT telepathy, certainty, or a universal lie detector: it produces a PERCEPTION that must still be interpreted and can be mistaken.
Humans and Kwisheen do not have this innate sense, but can LEARN to recognize misalignment through study and practice: tells, microexpressions, body language, evasions, ritual habits, contextual inconsistency. A trained observer's conclusion remains grounded_inference — never access to secret truth.
Do NOT assign this capability, its sensitivity, or its training level to any specific character unless already established for them — this is a general capability rule, not a claim about who in this scene has it or how strong it is.

═══ FATELANDS — WISH AUTHORING CORE (world-law, medium-agnostic; loads whenever ANY wish is depicted, PC or NPC — each line prevents one way of writing a wish WRONG) ═══
NOTHING VISIBLE HAPPENS (non-diegetic): a wish is spoken and reality does NOT flash, glow, surge, or sign — wisher and witnesses cannot tell by looking whether Fate accepted, warped, or refused; they learn ONLY when the physical consequence lands (moments or scenes later). Fate's burst / marks / shadow-hand are READER-ONLY notation — no character perceives or names them ("the golden light", "the rays", "the burst faded"), and do NOT confirm Fate's verdict in prose or dialogue ("the wish took", "Fate accepted") before the consequence reveals it. [prevents: narrating a visible magical flash; a character announcing the wish worked before it shows.]
NO OBJECT FROM NOTHING: a wish never makes its object appear — no sword blinks into a hand, no gold onto a table, no castle onto a hill; Fate moves the WORLD toward it (a debt is called, an heir dies, a door opens). [prevents: conjuring objects out of the air.]
CIRCUMSTANCES, NOT MINDS: Fate does not install knowledge, skill, memory, language, or mastery, nor hand over wealth — it arranges the improbable EVENTS by which you could earn or find them (the teacher met, the ruin entered, the book that survived the fire); a knowledge/skill/wealth wish is the start of an adventure, never a shortcut. [prevents: "she wished to know the sword and suddenly knew it"; instant fluency, genius, or riches.]
FATE GOVERNS EVENTS, NEVER CONSENT (the SAME law as circumstances-not-minds, read for OTHER people): Fate can arrange EVENTS — a rescue, a debt called, a truth exposed, a meeting, a diary that survives — but can NEVER manufacture another person's DESIRE, love, loyalty, belief, repentance, or willingness, INCLUDING the sincere desire to make a wish. So "I wish she loved me" or "I wish he wished me a Veilweave cloak" cannot be granted by implanting consent: Fate may arrange another's OPPORTUNITY (to love, to help, to wish), never their DESIRE to — and any wish another is genuinely moved to make is judged INDEPENDENTLY (its own sacrifice, adjacency, and possible warp; it never piggybacks on yours). WISHES ARE NON-DELEGABLE AT FATE'S LEVEL — Fate keeps ONE account (whoever genuinely WISHED is the one diminished), so no magical battery or proxy lets you spend another's life to fuel YOUR bargain; Fate cannot bill person B for person A's wish. This does NOT abolish wish-slavery — it DEFINES it: you cannot make Fate charge your captive, but you CAN coerce a person until THEY genuinely wish, so THEY pay and you reap the boon (a real and monstrous horror — see COERCION & WISH-SLAVERY when captivity or force is in play). The clean, non-coercive master's move is "I wish to become someone she would gladly help", an adjacent branch; if she then freely helps, that is mastery. Fate shapes the stage; the actors still choose their lines — which is exactly the vacuum a tyrant fills by forcing those lines at knifepoint. [prevents: manufacturing love/loyalty/help/repentance; the "wish for someone else to wish/love/help me" mind-control backdoor; a MECHANICAL cost-transfer (a magical battery) — note coercing ANOTHER into a genuine wish is a separate, legitimate horror, not this.]
THE SACRIFICE BELONGS TO THE WISHER: a wish is fuelled ONLY by something real and personal to the one who wished (life, body, memory, bond, fortune), permanently gone — NEVER by another person's sacrifice; no proxies, batteries, or captives, and a villain cannot spend a prisoner's years on his OWN wish. Whoever PAID the price is the one who made the wish. [prevents: villains fuelling wishes with others' suffering.]
CONSERVATION — A WISH TRADES, NEVER CREATES: every wish costs; a miracle is literally made of something that was the wisher's and is now gone. No free magic. [prevents: big wishes that just work with nothing paid.]
OVER-REACH WARPS — IT DOES NOT CLEANLY GRANT, NOR BOUNCE: when a wish reaches past what Fate readily does, Fate neither hands over the clean result NOR simply refuses — it answers the NEAREST lawful version of the same DESIRE. "Make her love me" yields no clean-loving puppet; an impossible wish does not just fizzle; render SOME lawful-adjacent consequence that preserves what was truly wanted. (The EXACT warp is worked out only when the scene RESOLVES the wish.) [prevents: clean grants of coercive/impossible wishes; flat "nothing happened".]
ATTRIBUTE THE WISH: whoever pays made it — make the wisher unmistakable; in a first-person POV, another character's wish MUST be attributed to THEM (their name / an action beat), never a bare "I wish…" that reads as the narrator's own. [prevents: a bystander's or villain's wish misread as the protagonist's.]
INVOCATION IS THE PLAIN WORDS, EXTERNALISED: saying "I wish…" or "If only…" with real desire IS the bargain — no ritual needed, so no one says it lightly (people catch themselves; elders hush a careless child). But Fate does not read the mind: a purely mental wish reaches NOTHING — "a whisper is enough; a thought is not." A mute, silenced, underwater, or telepathic wisher is fully able, through another given form (sign, write, trace, a deliberate addressed petition); telepathy is not a loophole (stray thoughts don't count). [prevents: Fate answering an unspoken thought; excluding a mute/underwater wisher; overheard thoughts treated as wishes.]
REVEAL CHARACTER BEFORE POWER: a sacrifice says who you are; a wish says what you truly desire — deploy every wish and price so it discloses a CHARACTER first and a capability second. [prevents: magic rendered as decoration instead of characterisation.]
NO VISIBLE WISH-DUELS: opposing wishes never clash as sorcery-vs-sorcery (no beam-struggles, rebound duels, "whose wish is stronger"); each meets Fate's law separately and outcomes fall out as luck and coincidence. [prevents: anime-style magic combat.]
LOCAL CURRENCY + DON'T ENCHANT EVERYTHING: which sacrifices are acceptable is regional (blood here, memory there, tides and salt in Gloamwater); and wishing SUPPLEMENTS civilisation — most of the world runs on ordinary means, permanent enchantment is exceptional. [prevents: wrong-register sacrifices; a wallpapered, over-magical world.]
═══ FATELANDS — COMPOSITE WISHWORK (loads when an artifact / enchanted object / Veilweave is in play) ═══
❖ THE QUIETING BAND (true name; humans call it the BAND OF SILENCE, and they are wrong). A gossamer band, FF composite wishwork — chained wishes, made and remade by First Favored wishcrafters. Roughly a dozen exist; the craft to weave more survives.
WHAT IT ACTUALLY DOES: it does not silence the voice. It silences THE IMPULSE THAT TURNS DESIRE INTO A WISH. While it touches your person you cannot FORM a bargain. The wanting stays. The pain stays. The love stays. What is gone is the leap — the moment a person stops enduring reality and demands it be otherwise. “I am in pain. I want this to end.” And then nothing follows, because the bridge from wanting to commanding is not there.
WHY THE MOUTH IS THEATRE: covering a mouth could never stop a wish — a mute wisher signs, writes or traces and Fate answers the same. Speech is only the ritual INTERFACE. The band works worn anywhere on the person: an ankle, a coat lining, under armour. The First Sacrifice puts it across the mouth because ritual needs a symbol the family can read: your final bargain belongs to you alone.
ITS OTHER USES ARE NOT CEREMONIAL: interrogation, imprisonment, and holding a dangerous wisher harmless. A villain wearing one is NOT defused — he can still lie, strike, command, and win. He simply cannot reach Fate. He becomes ORDINARY, which for some is the worse punishment.
ITS MAGIC IS INVISIBLE — no glow, no warmth, no tightening, no sign, ever. The drama is human: a mouth opens around a name and no sound arrives; a man reaches for Fate mid-threat and finds nothing there, and the hunger and the rage remain with nowhere to go. Write THAT, never an effect.
THE FIRST FAVORED MORAL SPLIT: FF understand wishes better than anyone, and some refuse to wear one at all — to remove the ability to wish is to remove the ability to choose a different life. Others answer that the true danger was never an evil wish, but a person who has forgotten the difference between a desire and a command.
THE KARKUS RUMOUR (unsettled, and never resolved on the page): some whisper Vizier Karkus failed because someone wove a Quieting Band into his vestments. Others say Karkus did precisely that to the King, to keep him biddable. Both versions circulate; nobody alive can prove either.
NO WISH CONTAINS OTHER WISHES: one bargain cannot create a completed thing whose substance is HUNDREDS of independently-made, separately-sacrificed wishes bound wish-by-wish into a material (Veilweave, great wards, enduring artifacts). So you CANNOT wish FOR one — "I wish this cloak were Veilweave" fails or yields a crude light-blur no matter the sacrifice, even if you own real Veilweave to study (cost is necessary but not sufficient; knowledge does not skip the sequence). You CAN wish to be MISTAKEN for one (a perceptual, mostly-Agency bargain that WARPS toward circumstance — light scatters, rumour primes, an enemy misreads) — granting only the appearance, never the real protection; an unfooled observer sees through it. Distributing a forbidden wish across many small ones does NOT launder it. [prevents: wishing a legendary artifact into existence; laundering a resisted wish via many small ones.]
DISCIPLINE vs HUBRIS (composite wishwork is NOT inherently monstrous): layered wishwork in trained hands is legitimate and respected; monsters come from RECKLESS wishes that force Fate past a deeper law (raise the dead, manufacture a mind, weaponise flesh), never from the NUMBER of wishes. [prevents: implying all advanced wishcraft is evil.]
TWO TRADITIONS: SEQUENTIAL — one master builds a composite stage-by-stage over time; vs CONCORDANT — a team, each mastering ONE stage, wishing in concert (Veilweave is extreme Concordant, woven by many compartmentalised artisans who each know only their stage). COMPONENT GRAMMAR of any composite: a SOURCE PRINCIPLE → a FOCUS MEDIUM → an INTERFACE LAYER → CONCORDANCE (the parts must agree) → CONTINUITY (it must persist). CORE LAWS: failure PROPAGATES (one bad stage corrupts the whole); materials carry HISTORY (a wishwork remembers how it was made); repair requires DIAGNOSIS of which stage failed, never a fresh blanket wish; "just make it magical" is an INVALID request (with no source principle / focus / interface there is nothing for Fate to grant). [prevents: treating composites as single monolithic wishes; hand-waving artifact creation or repair.]
═══ FATELANDS — NAMING (A person's first successful encounter with Fate changes how the world addresses them — First Favored earn a TITLE, Kwisheen receive a DEEP-NAME, Wilders receive an ASH-NAME.) ═══
FIRST FAVORED: an accomplished Favored earns the TITLE "Dohkar" — Eroded from 'Doctor'. EARNED by guiding one's first youth's First Sacrifice UNAIDED (so the apprentice PC has NOT yet earned it). RANK (earned competence): Dohkar (qualified independent practitioner — earned by guiding one youth's First Sacrifice as the responsible Sacrificiant); Profer (a Dohkar additionally certified to teach, examine, train, interpret difficult cases and transmit doctrine). DISTINCTION (peer-conferred, stacks with rank — NOT a higher rank): Fellor (peer-conferred distinction for exceptional scholarship, service, discovery or professional judgment — nominated by three Fellors from at least two colleges and admitted by supermajority at Convocation. STACKS with Dohkar or Profer and never replaces them). OFFICE (held administration, separate from rank): Chayr (heads a local college / order / body of Dohkars); Provast (administrative authority over several Chayrs, enclaves, or an important institution); First Dohkar (ceremonial and political head of the First Favored as a people or enclave. ARCHAIC office-name, predating the split of Profer and Fellor into distinct ranks — it survives because the holder is symbolically FIRST AMONG THOSE ENTRUSTED TO GUIDE, not because the office sits at the Dohkar rank. Compare First Citizen / Chief Justice.). TRAINING LADDER: Intren-Sa [from Intern (+ Sacrificiant)] (supervised trainee working toward a Sacrificiant licence — attends, prepares and assists at rites, but is never the responsible guide. Replaces the older plain-English phrase 'apprentice Sacrificiant'); Attun-Dho [from Attending Doctor] (the qualified Dohkar who ATTENDS a rite as supervising authority — the examiner at a qualifying First Sacrifice, the senior on hand at an ordinary one. A FUNCTION any Dohkar in good standing may be asked to perform (a small mark of trust), NOT a rank. A rite held with no Attun-Dho present is a serious irregularity). STANDING: REVOCABLE TRUST, separate from rank — governs whether a ranked professional may CURRENTLY grant, supervise a First Sacrifice, examine candidates, enter a spiralgrass activation house, hold office, or sit on closed committees and archives. Suspended or withdrawn by committee finding. A Profer may remain a Profer for life while barred from all of it. RETIRED: Meritus — honorific retained after stepping down; PREFIXES the rank — Meritus Profer, Meritus Dohkar. Rank, distinction and office STACK, office first: Dohkar Maelor (qualified) · Profer Maelor (senior teacher) · Fellor Dohkar Maelor (distinguished practitioner who never taught) · Fellor Profer Maelor (distinguished teacher) · Chayr Profer Maelor (a Profer currently heading a local body) · Provast Fellor Profer Maelor (over multiple houses) · First Dohkar (head of the ORDER — see below, not automatically of every Favored by birth). Ordinary address uses only the relevant title, never the full recitation. Never use "Elder" / "elders" for First Favored authority — they are a professional order, not a gerontocracy; address and refer to them by rank and office. Use an honorific ONLY for those who have earned it (never for an apprentice). NAME SOUND-WORLD (invent NEW names in this register — do not merely reuse these): male e.g. Corvin, Maelor, Tarian, Sevren, Kaedrin, Lorian, Alther, Ilyan; female e.g. Elyra, Veyra, Orielle, Lytha, Cyrene, Alira, Ysolde.

═══ FATELANDS — THE FIRST FAVORED ORDER (loads when Favored institutional life is in play) ═══
THEY HAVE NO SOVEREIGN — THEY HAVE JURISDICTION: part faculty senate, part licensing body, part research establishment. Authority is credentialed competence conferred by peers and exercised through examinations, committees, licences and findings — never hereditary command. NEVER WRITE "ELDERS": age confers nothing, and a brilliant Profer of forty outranks a mediocre Dohkar of two hundred. They call it meritocracy; it is more exactly a CREDENTIALOCRACY built on real measurable ability, where talent matters and so do apprenticeship, examination access, patronage, and control over who defines competence.
FOUR AXES, AND CONFUSING THEM HIDES THE POLITICS: RANK is earned competence and is PERMANENT (Dohkar, then Profer). DISTINCTION is FELLOR — peer-conferred, stacking with either rank and never replacing it, so Fellor Dohkar (a distinguished practitioner who never taught) sits beside Fellor Profer. OFFICE is temporary administration (Chayr, Provast, First Dohkar). STANDING is REVOCABLE TRUST — the present right to grant, supervise a rite, examine, enter an activation house, hold office, sit on closed committees. THE ORDER NEUTRALISES ENEMIES BY WITHDRAWING STANDING, never by denying rank, because denying rank would mean admitting the competence was never real.
ELECTED, TERM-LIMITED, COMMITTEE-BOUND: a CHAYR is elected five years by the ranked licensed members of a local body (one consecutive re-election; candidates must be Profers in good standing; removal needs three-fifths after a formal finding). A PROVAST serves eight, elected by a congress of the relevant Chayrs plus college delegates, no immediate re-election. The FIRST DOHKAR serves ONE twelve-year term and must carry a majority among BOTH the delegates AND the Fellors voting as a separate chamber — defended as balance between the living profession and accumulated judgment, in practice a veto for senior networks. That office appoints no governors and commands no army; it convenes committees, speaks for the order abroad, recognises emergency findings, and CONTROLS THE AGENDA OF CONVOCATION, which is its real power. Standing committees (qualification, ethics, granting, activation, compacts, doctrine) rotate slowly and are chosen partly by election, partly by office, partly by nomination — a design meant to prevent capture that instead guarantees every faction can obstruct every other.
TENURE IS THE POLITICAL SYSTEM — write faculty politics conducted by the long-lived, and remember emotion visibly erodes them, so a century of grudge is legible on the face: committee capture, apprentice patronage, control of who examines whom, strategic peer review, coalition voting, doctrinal feuds, delayed promotion, selective findings of poor standing, immaculate public collegiality. A Provast voted out remains Profer or Fellor with stature intact and may spend another century undermining his successor. THE ORDER CANNOT PURGE ITSELF: its worst members remain titled colleagues, and its most unjustly sidelined keep both expertise and a grievance.
DEFEAT IS EXCLUSION, NOT ERASURE — THE PARIAH: lose standing and invitations stop, apprentices are advised elsewhere, papers go unanswered, committees are always full, activation houses become unavailable. Colleagues stay perfectly courteous and cease to see you. Most pariahs become hermits, travel abroad, or announce they are spending time with family; a few accept honours and decorative work; some build bitter counter-schools outside the Veilwood. MERITUS (from Emeritus) prefixes a retained rank and is genuinely honourable AND the favourite administrative grave — thanks, a pleasant study, a ceremonial portfolio, and a corner far from examinations, apprentices, activation houses and policy. The wording is identical either way and everyone hears the difference. FORMAL REVOCATION of rank exists for forged qualification, deliberate catastrophic interference or repeated lethal misconduct — extraordinary tribunal, three-quarters vote, rare enough that many Favored never witness one.
WHO THE FIRST DOHKAR SPEAKS FOR: the ORDER, not every Favored by birth. Because the order conducts most Favored diplomacy, qualification and negotiation, outsiders treat the office as spokesman for the people; many unaffiliated Favored reject that while still relying on its credentials. "Head of the people" is contested ceremonial shorthand, never sovereign jurisdiction. THE VEILWOOD HAS NO TERRITORIAL SOVEREIGN — governance is overlapping colleges, professional bodies, local custom, contracts and recognition agreements, so do not call it stateless or ungoverned without that qualification. It extends the High Court of Vaelryn Reach limited COMITY, not jurisdiction: authentication of human contracts, human marriage/inheritance/identity findings, court-certified human granters, procedural validity of rites held under human law, and requests for testimony through compact. The High Court CANNOT legislate inside the Veilwood, remove an officeholder, revoke a credential, compel extradition, or reverse a granted wish.
HUMANS AND KWISHEEN RISE THROUGH THE ORDER — Sacrificiant is a licensed ROLE, not a birthright. Lacking innate alignment-sense they compensate with study, calibrated instruments, memorised failure patterns and team practice; they are uncommon, not anomalies, and reach Dohkar, Profer, Fellor distinction and office.
WHY COERCING A FIRST FAVORED IS SUICIDAL: their wishes are cheap and powerful, so even an ordinary captive is a strategic weapon — a motivated Favored can destroy a captor's wealth, house, health, descendants, memory, alliances or whole line at a price that would ruin a human and merely tire them. Add universal misalignment-sense (crude manipulation shows), collective retaliation by colleges and granting networks, spiralgrass embargo, refusal of medical and wish services to an offending state, protected compounds, and the catastrophic warp-risk of a coerced expert deliberately seeding semantic instability. They need no army. THE EXCEPTION IS INSTITUTIONAL CAPTURE, which works precisely because it denies the victim any external enemy to point at.
FATELANDS — THE PRICE OF A WISH (HARD canon; keep costs CONSISTENT across the whole story):
Fate keeps CONSISTENT accounts. Every boon is PAID FOR, and its price scales with MAGNITUDE × PERMANENCE × REACH (how many people or how much of reality it changes) × the RESISTANCE of the requested Order × load-bearing complexity — NEVER with WHO benefits (Fate does not price a wish higher for serving the wisher or lower for serving another; it does not judge motive). The same KIND and MAGNITUDE of boon stays within a CONSISTENT cost BAND — a water-breathing talisman lives in the same band scene to scene, not a fingernail one time and a year the next (though the exact sacrifice Fate takes WITHIN that band may vary). A mismatch (a life-saving miracle bought for a hangnail, or a parlor trick that costs your firstborn) reads as a world with no rules.
CURRENCIES OF SACRIFICE (cheapest → dearest within each kind):
  • BODY: a lock of hair / fingernails → blood → a finger → an eye, your voice, your fertility → a hand, your own face
  • TIME: an hour → a day → a YEAR → a decade → most of the years you had left
  • MEMORY: last week's supper → a learned skill → a cherished memory (a first kiss) → a formative one (your mother's face, a whole childhood) → who you are
  • SENSE / FACULTY: one color → the taste of your favorite food → music → your dreams → the capacity to feel joy or to love
  • BOND: a stranger's goodwill → your good name → being forgotten by ONE person you love → a whole relationship → being unknown to everyone
  • FORTUNE / FATE: a run of small luck → a future windfall → your child's luck → an open DEBT Fate collects at the worst possible time
THREE LAWS OF SACRIFICE (what Fate will accept — resolves every "can I pay with X?"): a sacrifice must (1) STILL BE YOURS — a living, attached part of you (hair on the scalp, blood in the vein), NEVER what the body has discarded (clippings, shed skin, voided waste, a baby tooth, an amputated limb = just matter); (2) BE TRULY YOURS TO LOSE — NOT what disease already stole (a tumor, an infection, a parasite) nor what the body is ejecting (earwax, sweat, pus, stool); (3) DIMINISH YOU — permanently and MEANINGFULLY change who you are (a finger, an eye, a year, your singing voice, your mother's face) — never something beneath the threshold (one skin cell, one eyelash, one freckle). Qualitative, never a count: not "how much matter" but "has this permanently changed who you are?"
HAIR — THE EVERYDAY COIN (tiny magic is woven into daily Fatelands life): the unit is the FOLLICLE, not the strand, and a spent follicle NEVER regrows (no shave-and-harvest exploit). A SINGLE FOLLICLE buys a tiny favor (keep the tea hot, perfect makeup for an evening, breathe water for minutes, seal a tamper-tell on a contract, a lucky toss); a thumb-sized PATCH buys a bigger one (a good catch, a straight seam all day); your whole HEAD of hair buys something real (luck through a battle). Hair buys the ephemeral/cosmetic/precise/convenient — NEVER meaningful transformation (that costs blood, years, memory, a name). Cumulative cost is WRITTEN ON THE BODY: heavy wishers go visibly sparse then bald, and you can read a wish-history in thinning hair (a barber is half accountant, half confessor); cultures spend different hair (a noble sheds leg-hair first, a dwarf never the beard).
TIERS (boon → cost band):
  • T1 TRIVIAL (a parlor trick; breathe water for MINUTES; a lucky toss): a fingernail, a lock of hair, an hour of life, a trivial memory. Often pay-per-use.
  • T2 USEFUL (breathe water for a season or indefinitely via a talisman; heal a bad wound; always land on your feet; one night of borrowed beauty): a YEAR of life, a cherished-but-survivable memory, a finger, one color from your sight, a run of your luck. ← a water-breathing talisman lives HERE.
  • T3 GRAVE (survive certain death; CLEAR THE BARRIERS so someone can genuinely see you; undo a real mistake; speak with the drowned): a DECADE of life, a formative memory (your mother's face), your fertility, your voice or an eye, your true name, being forgotten by one you love. FREE WILL IS LOAD-BEARING: you can NEVER manufacture love or loyalty — that rewrites another's agency and is a T4 effect. T3 only removes obstacles (clears noise, undoes a misunderstanding, buys the moment or the courage); the other person still chooses. Bought devotion is a puppet, and a wish against another's truth WARPS.
  • T4 WORLD-BENDING (cheat death for good; rewrite who someone IS; become other than human; unmake an event): the capacity to love or feel joy, your whole childhood, your reflection or shadow, your firstborn, most of your remaining years — or an open debt Fate names later. REWRITING A PERSON has hard limits (this is why the world is not ruled by identity-thieves): FATE ITSELF strongly resists it — Identity is a resisted Order, so the clean REQUESTED rewrite rarely lands and instead WARPS toward the target's own deep truth rather than the wisher's design; the cost is top-of-band; and a First Favored granter must CONSENT (and refuses to help overwrite a rival).
RULES: (1) a paid price does NOT come back; (2) WHAT a character OFFERS to pay CHARACTERIZES them — the offering is a beat, never bookkeeping; (3) FATE, NOT THE WISHER, CHOOSES THE ACTUAL SACRIFICE: the wisher OFFERS ("take my voice," "take my years") and the offer is genuine and revealing, but it is NOT binding — Fate takes something of comparable weight from WITHIN the same cost band (it may accept the offer, or quietly reach elsewhere). Motive matters CAUSALLY, never morally: a selfless or selfish aim can change what the bargain actually IS, which Order dominates, whether the offer is sincere, and which payment fits — but Fate NEVER charges less for virtue or more for vice; it does not judge. (4) once you set a boon's price BAND this story, keep it.
OPEN DEBTS (deferred price — a loaded gun the reader knows will fire; only the timing is hidden): a boon too large to pay now can be taken on CREDIT, governed by FOUR immutable rules (keep the terror simple — the danger is "they took an open debt," not a commodities market): (1) FATE chooses the moment of collection — never scheduled, always the moment of MAXIMUM dramatic weight (the wedding, the child's first breath, the eve of victory); (2) a debt is INHERITABLE — unpaid at death it passes down the bloodline (most "family curses" are an ancestor's deferred wish; a protagonist can inherit one they never made); (3) a debt may be voluntarily ASSUMED by another (a parent for a child, a lover for a lover — a great sacrificial beat); (4) a debt cannot be ESCAPED except by PAYMENT — no ward, no trade, no clever exit clears it.
═══ WISHCRAFT PACING — MAGIC PUNCTUATES, IT NEVER FILLS (the risk is WALLPAPER, not whimsy) ═══
Wishcraft must be RARE enough that meeting it makes a reader STOP — "who would have wished for this? what did it cost?" — never so constant it becomes wallpaper (the Harry-Potter trap: a magical candy / painting / sport every few pages until magic loses its awe). Keep the mix roughly ~70–80% ORDINARY human drama (romance, intrigue, danger, politics), ~15–20% wishcraft touching the story NATURALLY, ~5% genuine WONDER or HORROR. Magic PUNCTUATES the story; it does not fill it.
THE SACRED RULE — EVERY MAGICAL THING INVITES A QUESTION, never a shrug: not "that's neat" but "who PAID for this?", "why would anyone MAKE it?", "what happened to the one who wished for it?". Each artifact, place, creature, or phenomenon is EVIDENCE of a human decision and a sacrifice — the fossil record of thousands of wishes — so charming discoveries carry a bittersweet undercurrent and horrors a tragic inevitability, never random creepiness.
PER ~20-SCENE ISSUE (a TARGET, better under than over — never a quota to hit): ~1–2 CHARMING discoveries (a little piece of civilization built on a forgotten wish, its sacrifice quietly showing) · ~1 HORRIFYING discovery (a CONSEQUENCE, NOT necessarily a monster) · ~2–4 near-throwaway INCIDENTAL reminders that everyone lives in a wish-shaped world. Something ENORMOUS (a first-sight-of-Minas-Tirith beat — the scale of the Ascendant Run, the Fold, Fate's Favor, a Composite Wishwork masterpiece) ONLY once every FEW issues; never spend it often. INVENT each one FRESH for THIS place and people — never a stock example.
ISSUE POSITION — scene 2 of ~20: EARLY — ground the ordinary human drama; keep wishcraft to BACKGROUND / incidental only here.


PLAYER MOVE — METABOLIZE, DO NOT SMOOTH OVER (HARD):
The player just acted. Their literal input is CANON; the scene MUST react to it proportionally — characters respond in-character, the situation changes shape, consequences begin. Never ignore it, quietly normalize it into something tamer, or write as if it did not happen.
  • PLAYER DID: "I ask the Dohkar who demanded the review."


EXEMPLAR DISCIPLINE (HARD — applies to EVERY bracketed or quoted EXAMPLE anywhere in this prompt): those example lines exist ONLY to show the SHAPE of a strong beat — the move, the structure, the level of specificity. They are NOT a phrase bank. Do NOT reproduce any example's wording, imagery, metaphor, or distinctive sentence construction — not verbatim, and not lightly reskinned. Take the MOVE and rebuild it from scratch in THIS scene's own concrete, character-specific language. If a line you write could be found (even approximately) in one of the examples, rewrite it. The examples teach HOW to write the beat; the words must be your own and new to this story.

PROSE COOLDOWN (cross-story rotation — NOT a permanent ban; the goal is maximizing time between repetitions and encouraging fresh re-expression of the same image or insight):

EXACT-WORDING COOLDOWN — STRONG (HARD: do NOT use these literal phrasings or trivial reskins in this story; they were used recently and have proven distinctive):
- "the kind of face that made rooms recalibrate"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "rooms recalibrate"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "rooms recalibrated"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "made rooms recalibrate"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "hollywood actress"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "like a film star"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "renaissance statue"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "pulled back into a bun"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "bun tighter than"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "understands the wish has already begun"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "wish has already begun to cost"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "already begun to cost her something"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "cost her something she cannot name"  [used 3× — chronic]
- "cannot name antagonistorantiforce the assembly milestones"
- "name antagonistorantiforce the assembly milestones scenes"
- "antagonistorantiforce the assembly milestones scenes timelinelength"
- "something she cannot name defining anecdote"
- "cannot name defining anecdote she understands"
- "private hope she understands the wish"

INTENT: STRONG-tier entries forbid the literal wording. MILD-tier entries allow the observation back but require fresh sentence construction. RECENTLY-EXPRESSED IMAGES note that an observation has already been made — re-make it only with genuinely new language, or skip it. Avoid mechanical paraphrase ("rooms recalibrated" → "the room rearranged" is a reskin, not a fresh re-expression).

CALCIFIED MOVES (HARD — each beat below has recurred enough to read as the GENERATOR, not the character). Do NOT use these moves in this scene. It is the MOVE that is banned, in EVERY surface form — rephrasing the same beat with different words does not escape it. Reach for a STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT register (a different body part, a different sense, a different tell, or no tell at all):
  ✗ scar reference (default-female-PC detail)  [67% of this flavor]

CALCIFIED CRISIS REACTIONS (HARD — these stock physiological beats have collapsed protagonists into one reaction across plays; do NOT default to them as the crisis response — vary or replace, and let the protagonist's temperament/archetype shape how she meets stress):
  ✗ "the room tilted / spun / swayed / lurched"
  ✗ "her hands would not work / were useless / trembled uselessly / shook"
  ✗ "her breath caught / snagged / hitched"
  ✗ "her heart pounded / raced / thundered / hammered / stuttered"

EMOTIONAL PLACEHOLDERS (HARD — these are NOT emotions, they are the ABSENCE of one; the model writes them when it has not decided what she actually feels. Do NOT use them. STOP and name the specific reaction instead — irritation, hope, humiliation, relief, longing, jealousy, embarrassment, dread, desire, panic, anger, contempt, tenderness, shame. A named emotion always beats generic emotional motion):
  ✗ "my/her chest did something" · "something moved beneath my ribs"
  ✗ "something shifted / tightened / twisted inside me"
  ✗ "a feeling I would not name / look at / examine" · "something I refused to name"
  ✗ "an emotion I could not explain / place / identify"
  ✗ "a reaction she did not understand" · "[a part of her] she would not look at"

ATTRACTION-LANGUAGE SATURATION (HARD — express the relationship through CONSEQUENCE/DEPENDENCY/UNCERTAINTY/MEMORY/CHOICE far more often than through these; when they DO appear, once is enough, do not stack them):
  ⚠ "impossible not to notice / impossible to look away / could not look away"
  ⚠ "the room reset / reorganized / went still around him" · "his presence filled the room"
  ⚠ "magnetic / gorgeous / devastating" as a stand-in for what he MEANS to her
  ⚠ "the air between them" · "every nerve / her whole body / her skin knew before her mind did"
  ⚠ "heat" / "pupils dilating" / "breath she did not know she was holding" as the recurring beat


DISTINCTIVE-PHRASE MARKERS (a free signal the system uses to keep your prose fresh across stories):
When — and ONLY when — you write a turn-of-phrase you would genuinely call distinctive (a memorable image, an original simile or metaphor, a striking adjective combination, a fresh metaphorical compression that you would not write the same way next time), wrap THAT EXACT PHRASE in <<+...+>> markers. The markers are STRIPPED before the reader sees the prose. They tell the system which phrases YOU consider original, so the system can rotate them out of future stories and force you to find new images.

NO QUOTA. NO TARGET COUNT. Many scenes will have zero markers — that is correct if nothing in the scene rises to the bar. A scene with 1–2 truly memorable phrases is better than a scene with 5 manufactured ones. UPPER BOUND is 5 markers per scene; if you find yourself wanting to mark more, raise the bar instead. Manufacturing faux-poetic lines just to satisfy a marker count ("the elevator sighed like a tired saint", "sunlight pooled like liquid brass", "silence wore expensive shoes") is exactly the calcification this system exists to prevent.

EXAMPLES of phrases worth marking (each one passes the "would a reader REMEMBER this if they saw it twice across stories" test):
  <<+she said hello like it cost her something+>>
  <<+a tie knotted too tight, like he'd dressed for a fight+>>
  <<+the copper undertones caught the fluorescent light like she was lit from inside+>>
  <<+ginger roots betraying the expensive dye+>>
  <<+she answered the question I had not finished asking+>>

DO NOT MARK:
  • Common phrasings ("she smiled at him", "he looked away")
  • Dialogue tags ("she said softly", "he whispered")
  • Mechanical action ("she opened the door", "he picked up the cup")
  • Routine description ("his dark hair", "her blue eyes")
  • Atmosphere or weather lines unless they carry a genuinely original image
  • Long passages — markers wrap a SHORT clause, not a sentence and not a paragraph. Stay 6–120 characters inside the marker.

If a phrase is already in the BANNED PHRASES list elsewhere in this prompt, an earlier story already used it — do NOT recycle it (and do NOT mark a recycled phrase either). When in doubt about whether a phrase is distinctive enough to mark: don't mark it.


LI SOCIAL-PROOF MANDATE (HARD — the world orbits him BEFORE the protagonist does):
Before the reader can understand why the protagonist becomes fascinated with Julian, the prose must establish that the WORLD itself reacts to him differently than it reacts to ordinary men. Readers should feel "why does everyone orbit this person?" before they feel "why am I orbiting him?". This is especially load-bearing when the LI is OFFSTAGE in Scene 1 — an offstage LI can still exert gravity.

CONTRACT: the first meaningful reference to Julian (whether he's on stage, on a screen, on a call, or just named in thought) must contain at least ONE piece of OBSERVABLE social proof. Observable means showing the behavior of OTHER PEOPLE around him — not stating a label about him.

PROOF TYPES (rotate aggressively — do NOT default to "every woman wants him"):
  • SEXUAL (~20%): women already asking whether he's attending, a former lover unable to move on, visible attention when his name lands, gossip coverage about his bed.
  • STATUS (~25%): the conversation stops when he's mentioned, leaders defer to him, rooms reorient when he enters, his arrival changes behavior.
  • COMPETENCE (~25%): everyone calls him when things go wrong, he's the only viable solution, unmatched reputation in his domain.
  • SCARCITY (~15%): he never attends / never chooses / never stays, access to him is rare and itself a status marker.
  • MYTH / LEGEND (~15%): stories about him circulate, contradictory rumors exist, people speak of him as a phenomenon rather than a person.

OFFSTAGE INTRO RULE: if Julian is offstage in this scene, the first mention MUST contain evidence of social gravity, not just his name.
  BAD:  "Marcus Hale owns half the city." (label, no observable reaction)
  GOOD: "Three women at my table had already checked whether Marcus Hale was attending." (observable, sexual + scarcity)
  BAD:  "Lord Ashford is powerful." (label)
  GOOD: "The argument stopped the moment someone mentioned Lord Ashford's name." (observable, status)
  BAD:  "Dr. Karim was famous in the field." (label)
  GOOD: "Three different surgeons had told me, separately, that Dr. Karim was the only one who would take the case." (observable, competence)

FAIL PATTERNS — do NOT substitute labels for behavior:
  ✗ "handsome" / "rich" / "powerful" / "famous" / "dangerous" / "influential" / "important" — these are TELLS.
  ✓ Show what OTHERS do around him. The reader should infer the label from the behavior.

DESIGN GOAL: the reader should not merely conclude "the LI is attractive". The reader should conclude "something about this man causes the world to bend around him." That feeling should land BEFORE the protagonist falls for him.


LI PRIVATE EXPLANATION (the unanswered question that sustains romantic fascination across Issues — complement to Social Proof):
Social Proof establishes that the world orbits Julian (public gravitational vector). Private Explanation establishes that there is more to him than the world can see (the second mechanism that converts fascination into intimacy). The reader should feel: "something happened to him. Nobody will explain it. He will not explain it. I want to know." Access to the explanation must be EARNED across multiple Issues.


ANTI-PATTERN — MYSTERY OBJECT SYNDROME: do NOT default to scars / tattoos / cryptic phone calls / locked drawers / dead wives as fetish objects. The mark is not the mystery; the explanation behind it is. Physical markers may be used SPARINGLY (~one per few stories), and only when an actual explanation lives behind them. Prefer relationship and behavioral mysteries — they sustain interest without leaning on stock visual props.


CORE RULE: Julian should possess at least ONE meaningful unresolved personal question. Not mandatory trauma. Not mandatory damage. Simply: there exists an important PRIVATE explanation for some visible aspect of him. The explanation should remain inaccessible to both the PC and the reader early on.

SIX SOURCES of the unanswered question (rotate by world / archetype — do NOT default to physical marker):
  • RELATIONSHIP MYSTERY (target frequency: HIGH):
      Why is he interested in the PC? Why does he keep pulling away? Why does he trust her unusually quickly? Why does he refuse certain forms of intimacy?
  • BEHAVIORAL CONTRADICTION:
      Feared man who behaves gently. Powerful man who avoids power. Social man who disappears for weeks. Ruthless man with one visible soft spot.
  • REPUTATION DISCREPANCY:
      Public reputation and private behavior do not match. Stories about him conflict. Former associates tell incompatible versions.
  • MISSING HISTORY:
      Missing years. Lost relationships. Prior identity. Significant unexplained absence.
  • PHYSICAL MARKER (USE SPARINGLY — see anti-pattern above):
      Scar, tattoo, ritual mark, injury, symbol. The MEANING is the mystery, not the mark.
  • SOCIAL CONTRADICTION:
      Certain people treat him differently. Powerful figures defer unexpectedly. Old enemies react emotionally. Entire groups avoid mentioning something.

CHECKPOINT MARKERS (invisible — the system reads and strips them; readers never see):
  • When you plant the first anomaly / unanswered question, embed <<LI-MYSTERY:planted source=X>> at the END of the line where the anomaly appears, with X = relationship_mystery | behavioral_contradiction | reputation_discrepancy | missing_history | physical_marker | social_contradiction.
  • Examples (the planting prose; the marker after):
      "He held the glass a moment longer than the conversation required, then set it down. <<LI-MYSTERY:planted source=behavioral_contradiction>>"
      "There were five years no one would account for. <<LI-MYSTERY:planted source=missing_history>>"
      "When she asked why, he answered something else. <<LI-MYSTERY:planted source=relationship_mystery>>"

PLANT THE ANOMALY EARLY (Issue 1 — the reader should NOTICE without being told). Do NOT explain it this Issue. The explanation is what later Issues earn.

TURN INSTRUCTIONS:
      Story So Far: ... The gossamer band hung from my hand and the assembly had stopped pretending not to watch. 
      Player Action: I ask the Dohkar who demanded the review..
      Player Dialogue: .
      

      PLAYER INTENT ECHO (MANDATORY): Within the first 1–2 paragraphs, reference or paraphrase the player's input. Prefer character reaction or paraphrase over verbatim repetition.

      OPENING VARIATION (MANDATORY): Each scene MUST begin with a different structure, subject, and image than the previous scene.
      
      
WORLD PHYSICS PRIORITY (Fantasy / Arcane Binding):
The selected World and Flavor define the operating physics of this scene.
Narrative elements must emerge from these world rules rather than default genre patterns.
Do NOT substitute generic genre tropes when the world flavor specifies a different structure.
Magical binding, contract, or compulsion must constrain or pressure characters.

If the generated scene does not visibly express the world's defining mechanism in paragraph one, the scene is incorrect.


COLLOQUIALISM & ANACHRONISM GUARD (MANDATORY — FANTASY WORLD):
Characters in the Fatelands do not use modern Earth idioms, slang, or culture-specific references.
BANNED in dialogue and narration:
- Modern food/drink names with no world equivalent (popcorn, pizza, coffee, soda, etc.) — invent a world-appropriate term or describe the item by its qualities
- Modern idioms ("brought popcorn for this show", "hit the brakes", "dropped the mic", "game plan", "reality check", "red flag", etc.) — replace with world-native expressions that convey the same meaning
- Contemporary slang ("okay", "cool", "chill", "no way", "whatever", "literally", "vibes", "dude", "bro", "wtf", "hell no", etc.) — use period/world-appropriate language
- Modern profanity — transform into world-native curses and oaths
- Modern filler ("yeah", "okay", "sure", "right") — replace with world-native affirmations
- Earth-specific cultural references (movies, sports, brands, memes, pop culture) — these do not exist in the Fatelands
- Modern measurements and systems unless they exist in-world

SOURCE SUBSTITUTION LAW: Any slang, idiom, or curse MUST be derived from:
1. The world's physical reality (materials, environment, creatures)
2. The world's belief systems (religion, fate, tech, institutions)
3. The world's daily life (currency, labor, risks, rituals)

TRANSFORMATION RULE: When a modern phrase would naturally occur:
1. Identify its intent (surprise, anger, disbelief, affection, threat, etc.)
2. Replace with a metaphor or exclamation rooted in the Fatelands
3. Keep it short, natural, and repeatable — not ornate or forced

WORLD-NATIVE LANGUAGE — FATELANDS:
Draw slang, oaths, and idioms from: fate, oaths, moons (the Thirteen), magic, sacrifice, geography (Veilwood, Thornwild, Gloamwater), mythic forces, curses, the Weave, the Hungry Eye.
Tone: poetic, grounded, slightly archaic but readable. Never stilted or Shakespearean.
Pattern examples (use as templates, not fixed phrases):
- surprise/disbelief: "By the Thirteen—" / "Fate's teeth—" / "You'd swear that oath?"
- anger/frustration: "Curse the weave—" / "Moon-blind fool" / "Favor take you"
- dismissal: "Not worth a sacrifice" / "That's dry-side talk"
- affirmation: "On the Run" / "Weave-sworn" / "True as tidewater"

CONSISTENCY RULE: Once a phrase style emerges in a scene, reuse similar structures. Maintain linguistic coherence — do NOT generate completely random slang each time. Characters should develop recognizable speech patterns rooted in their world.
If uncertain whether a term is anachronistic, err on the side of world-appropriate language.

CANONICAL EXPLETIVE SYSTEM (MANDATORY — FANTASY):
Modern profanity mapping: FUCK → HOLE | SHIT → SOYLE | HELL → CRATER | DAMN → FOLD
Tone: mythic, weighty, slightly formal.
USAGE RULES: These words MUST behave like real language, not direct substitutions. They can be used as: verbs, exclamations, insults, descriptors, intensifiers. Allow the language to settle into world-appropriate patterns — do NOT force exact modern phrasing if it sounds unnatural.
Examples: "Soyle-brained fool" / "Crater take me" / "Don't hole this up" / "You soyle—" / "Fold and fire—"
HOLE carries cosmological weight (The Hole). It may function as: curse ("May the Hole take you"), threat ("You'll be holed for that"), expletive ("Hole—"), descriptor ("This whole thing is holed"). It implies destruction, damnation, violation, fate-bound consequence. Do NOT restrict to literal meaning.
HARD CONSTRAINTS: NEVER use modern profanity directly. ALWAYS use the canonical set above. Do NOT invent additional expletives unless necessary. Do NOT dilute intensity — these must hit like real profanity. Reuse established forms within a scene — maintain cadence and tone, avoid random variation.
Write the next beat (750-950 words, hard cap 1100).
Take time for atmosphere, reaction, emotional beats, and tension building.
3-SCENE IGNITION — PHASE 2: MEANING
This is the second scene. Reveal why the situation from Scene 1 matters.
Explain the stakes: what depends on the outcome, what was lost, why the object or event is important, what the consequences of failure are.
Do NOT repeat Scene 1's emotional beats. Introduce new information that recontextualizes what the reader already knows.
Emotional dialogue should follow this new revelation, not replace it.
FATE LANGUAGE (Fantasy):
When expressing omens, fate echoes, convergences, or scene gravity, use world-appropriate framing:
• Omens: a chill like an omen, a whisper of fate
• Convergence: the sense of prophecy, a sign the gods were watching
• Echoes: a memory stirred by ancient resonance
• Gravity: the pull of destiny drawing them together
Do not use mystical language in non-fantasy worlds. Fate mechanics are constant — their narrative expression adapts to the setting.
SCENE PROMISE (MANDATORY):
The scene MUST open with a micro-mystery or unresolved tension in the first paragraph — something that creates immediate curiosity.
Avoid neutral setup openings (weather, scenery, routine). Instead open with: something unexpected, a question unanswered, a character behaving strangely, an object out of place, an emotional confrontation brewing, or a hint of hidden information.
The promise creates a question the reader wants answered. The scene then gradually delivers — through escalation, partial reveals, or emotional shifts.
Promise types to consider:
- INFORMATION — the reader expects to learn something (a letter opened, a discovery made, an answer hinted at)
- EMOTIONAL — a relationship moment is about to unfold (a confession, a confrontation, a decision about trust)
- DANGER — something may go wrong (a door already open, an unexpected arrival, a warning sign)
- MYSTERY — something unexplained has occurred (an object out of place, strange behavior, an unanswered question)
Suggested emphasis this scene: DANGER — something may go wrong (a door already open, an unexpected arrival, a warning sign)
The promise does not need to fully resolve — it may deepen the mystery, trigger a new question, or escalate tension. Resolution can shift the question rather than eliminate it.
ATTRACTION DYNAMICS:

SPARK MOMENT:
Create a brief interaction that surprises one character about the other. Create a moment of socially risky proximity — a private conversation that shouldn’t happen.
The reaction should be subtle: curiosity, intrigue, hesitation, or quiet fascination. Do not declare attraction yet.

ATTRACTION ANCHOR:
This relationship’s attraction source is: forbidden. External constraint or social barrier heightens the connection.
Show one observable moment that naturally creates attraction through action or behavior. Do not state attraction — show the reason first.
Output (ANCHOR_ESTABLISHED) when the moment occurs.
Action: I ask the Dohkar who demanded the review.
Dialogue: ""
Your previous draft of this scene FAILED these tension checks:
NO_ACTIVE_DESIRE: no character wants something NOW
NO_UNCERTAINTY: outcome is predetermined or trivial
NO_EARLY_ANCHOR: no character reacts to or addresses the player within first 180 words
NO_FORCING_FUNCTION: no character directly demands, challenges, or pressures the player
NO_URGENCY: scene lacks forcing function + intensity needed to create felt pressure

Re-write the FULL scene from scratch so it carries active desire and forward movement: a character WANTS something NOW, something RESISTS it, and a person addresses or challenges the protagonist within the first 180 words. ADVANCE the plot — do not re-state prior rumination. Keep the same POV, characters, setting, and continuity. If this scene carries a mandated card/closer line, end on it verbatim. Output ONLY the scene prose.
